Urban Lights & Gucci Nights: An Exclusive Look Inside the LACMA Art + Film Gala

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

The night was young. Very young, as I cruised across Los Angeles to this year’s LACMA Art + Film Gala. I decided to arrive before the usual red-carpet mayhem starts, and because I had gotten ready several hours too early, I was starting to entertain the idea of having a quick nap in my glam and gown. I was wearing a 1930s purple satin bias-cut dress that I won at an auction for considerably less than it was worth. Such a purchase can do wonders for one’s self-esteem: I felt like a million bucks.

This year marked the 15th anniversary of the gala, which honors artists and filmmakers while raising funds to support the museum’s programming. The evening, sponsored by Gucci and co-chaired by Eva Chow and Leonardo DiCaprio, would celebrate Latvian artist Vija Celmins and filmmaker Denis Villeneuve. The art world and Hollywood are not always natural bedfellows, although they have certainly become more comfortable with one another over the years. I was interested to see how the two would behave over dinner.

I arrived fashionably early, both because I am extremely punctual and partly because I have developed an aversion to the chaos that accompanies certain Los Angeles events. Anya Taylor-Joy was making her way towards the photographers in an archival Pierre Cardin sculptural gown and jacket looking like a beautiful futuristic loofah. I spotted the sparkle of her Tiffany & Co. Schlumberger Stars and Moons necklace before I even registered who was wearing it. The necklace was doing its own job of announcing her.

As she approached the carpet, which was lined with photographers and press, I was surprised that nobody was yelling. The atmosphere was remarkably calm, a far cry from some of the movie premiere assignments I’ve attended, where you can barely hear yourself think over the screaming of fans and photographers. Behind the carpet was the backdrop that has become something of a celebrity in its own right: Chris Burden’s Urban Light, the monumental installation made up of more than 200 restored cast-iron antique street lamps. It is a Los Angeles sight that never loses its magic.

In the distance, I heard a woman having a meltdown with security. “Am I not famous enough?” she yelled.

I never established what the issue was. Perhaps she had been denied access to the carpet, or she simply wanted to take a photograph and was being told no. Either way, she was making a scene in sparkles. I hurried past the publicists who were watching with mild horror. “Never a dull moment,” I said. One or two laughed as I continued inside.

During cocktail hour, I stopped to say hello to Ed Ruscha, whom I had met a few nights earlier at a birthday party. He would be seated at Larry Gagosian’s table that evening. I made my way around the room, passing Julia Garner, who is invariably one of the nicest people in it. She was wearing Loewe with sculptural gold earrings. She always asks me if I’m an actress, which makes me blush slightly, despite the fact that I have never once given her reason to believe that I am. Nearby, Iris Law was wearing a beautiful pearlescent tiled dress by a brand she told me was called Amaramara. Up close, the dress looked almost as though it had been constructed from mother-of-pearl shells. She explained that it was lined in leather, making it surprisingly soft against the skin.

I walked by a cluster of familiar faces, getting stepped on a few times along the way—a long train is lovely in theory, but less so in a crowded room full of people who have somewhere to be. I spent much of the evening trying to protect my almost 100-year-old archival satin from people’s shoes. Artist Alex Israel told me he was particularly excited to see Rosé perform later that evening. I spotted Jonas Wood in the distance, along with Lauren Halsey, who had recently created the most extraordinary art direction and stage design for Erykah Badu at the Hollywood Bowl. I had been lucky enough to see it, and Halsey had projected her artwork across the venue, making the entire experience feel as though you had stepped directly into her world.

I also ran into Jordan Firstman, whose film Club Kid had just come out. We heard about a rather unexpected screening, which included Oprah. Apparently, she wasn’t entirely familiar with the film’s more specialized vocabulary and required a brief introduction to gay culture, including what a popper was and what people meant by G. (I would have loved to be there for that.)

Meanwhile, waiters were circulating with bite-sized beef tenderloin and miso black cod. I attempted to be a fly on the wall and observe who was talking to whom. Many of the faces I didn’t recognize were presumably museum patrons. I always find it interesting trying to distinguish between those who are famous and those who are simply very rich. At a gala like this, the two categories overlap quite considerably.

Eventually, bells rang and we were ushered across the museum towards the dining room. On the way, I spotted Jennifer Tilly, whom I am a big fan of. I complimented her Gucci ensemble and told her I admired her JAR jewelry collection. She immediately asked if I wanted a selfie. It turned out to be one of my favorite photographs of the night.

Jennifer told me that her dress had been five sizes too large the evening before, but that the Gucci elves had worked their magic and made it fit just right. She was wearing a Jean Schlumberger Hedges and Flowers necklace for Tiffany & Co., with matching earrings from the 1960s. It is one of his most iconic designs, and she lit up as I asked her about it. I found myself thinking that I would happily abandon the rest of the evening to order a martini with her and gossip. She was delightful.

I also introduced myself to another Real Housewife I have a soft spot for, Kathy Hilton, who told me she was wearing vintage Gucci. The bow at her collar looked Edwardian-inspired.

The dining room was arranged with extraordinarily long tables with crisp white tablecloths and florals, giving the feeling of a very glamorous wedding. I was seated with Devon Lee Carlson, who was wearing a sheer Gucci number. Model Alex Consani came over to share some tea with us, and we attempted to make our way to the bar before being gently redirected. The speeches were about to begin, apparently, and the time for wandering was over.

The museum’s director introduced Eva Chow, who announced that this would be her final year helping to put on the gala. After years of bringing together the art and film communities, it was a rather poignant moment.

At a neighboring Gucci table sat François-Henri Pinault and his wife, Salma Hayek Pinault, in a sequin red gown by Gucci. Her waist was so dramatically cinched that she looked as though she had stepped straight out of an eighteenth-century portrait. Further along were Gucci creative director Demna, Kim Kardashian in a Bordeaux-hued sparkling gown, and Viola Davis, who was glowing. I also spotted 2hollis, who confessed that he didn’t know anyone there, so Devon and I immediately offered to adopt him for the evening.

There was also an impressive coterie of Victoria’s Secret Angels, including Elsa Hosk, Candice Swanepoel, and Rosie Huntington-Whiteley. Candice wore a Gucci gown held together by what appeared to be several strategically placed crystal strings in front of her crotch. Rosie had opted for a rather more revealing arrangement including a prominent display of her derrière with the Gucci logo doing some heavy lifting when it came to concealing her modesty. Later, I found several of the models outside smoking. Even angels need a cigarette.

In the bathroom, a woman in sky-high heels and a very tight Gucci gown was applying her lipstick. As she turned to leave, her clutch slipped from her hands and burst open on the floor. Its contents scattered in every direction, rolling underneath the neighboring stalls. Lipstick, a wallet, what I believe was a fake nail, and some medication. I admired her composure as she collected everything, composed her dignity, and returned to the party. The show must go on.

Back in the dining room, I said hello to my friend Noor Alfallah and Al Pacino. They were seated with Millicent Wilner, the senior director of Gagosian’s London gallery, who is one of my favorite people to run into every few months. On my way back to my seat, I passed Parker Posey, dressed in the most beautiful kaftan dripping in crystals, and I told her she looked gorgeous. By this point, I had begun mentally assembling my own fantasy dinner party featuring Jennifer Tilly, Parker Posey, and Kathy Hilton.

Eventually, the lights dimmed and the room fell quiet. A short film spotlighted Vija Celmins and her work. Based in New York, the artist spoke about her enduring fascination with the California coastline, the ocean, and the moon. She also mentioned that she had attended UCLA and, amusingly, had taken nothing from the experience. The room laughed at the accomplished artist delivering such an assessment, before she took the stage to accept the prestigious honor.

Next came Nicole Kidman, wearing a Chanel runway gown that immediately brought to mind Edgar Degas’s Little Dancer Aged Fourteen (1881). She spoke about Denis Villeneuve, admitting that while she has never actually appeared in any of his films, she’s an enormous admirer of his. “A few simple words and an image can stay with you long after the screen goes black,” she said.

We watched a montage of Villeneuve’s films, including Prisoners, Sicario, Blade Runner 2049, Arrival, Incendies and, of course, Dune. As someone who considers Sicario one of her all-time favorite films, I was particularly pleased to be there for this one.

When Villeneuve took the stage, he spoke about the importance of art in an age increasingly shaped by artificial intelligence. Images, he explained, had always spoken to him more profoundly than words. He recalled a visit to LACMA where the work of James Turrell served as inspiration for his film Arrival. Looking back, it is easy to see the connection: The enormous, mysterious spacecraft in the film has something of the quality of a Turrell light installation.

“Some artists use a paintbrush, I use a film crew,” he said.

It was a wonderful line, but his larger point was more affecting. He spoke about the importance of continuing to make art, particularly now, and about wanting to pass that belief on to his children.

Seated nearby were Timothée Chalamet and members of the casts of his films. I found myself looking around the room and thinking about the extraordinary concentration of talent gathered in one place. Vija Celmins, Denis Villeneuve, Al Pacino, Ed Ruscha: These are people who have spent their lives taking creative risks and changing the way we see things. There was something rather moving about sitting among them. For all the sequins, diamonds, and elaborate gowns, this was ultimately an evening celebrating people who had devoted themselves to making something that didn’t exist before.

After dinner, we were invited back outside for Rosé’s performance. Paris Hilton introduced her with an enthusiastic declaration of “That’s hot!” followed by “Sliving,” which felt like an appropriate transition from the intellectual rigors of the evening into the after-dark revelry. As we made our way outside, servers circulated with cotton candy, meringues, and miniature metal cups of caramel corn.

Devon and I made our way across Urban Light one last time, taking photographs beneath the lamps. She taught me how to use Burst mode on my phone, which was the most useful thing I learned all evening.

There were whispers of an after-party at Eva Chow’s house. I briefly considered going, then weighed the prospect against the fact that I was tired, had already spent several hours in a corseted state of social alertness, and was supposed to go home and write this very piece. Thankfully I have grown out of the kind of FOMO that comes with knowing a party is happening somewhere in Los Angeles without you. I headed home to crawl in bed with my cat.

On the drive back, I found myself thinking about Villeneuve’s speech. It is easy to get swept up in the spectacle of an evening like this. The clothes, the jewelry, the famous faces, the endless choreography of who is sitting with whom. But every so often, you’re reminded why these people are interesting in the first place.

After his speech, I will make it a point to see the final installment of Dune next month, and I can’t wait to see what he creates next. For now, I had a purple satin dress to hang up, photographs to look through, and a story to write. I fell asleep thinking about art, ambition, and all the things I might make if I were brave enough. All in due time.

François-Henri Pinault, Salma Hayek Pinault

Charles Porch, Aurora James, Robert Denning

Les Amazones reçues 5 sur 5

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Désormais seules en tête de l'Arkema Seconde Ligue, les Amazones se déplaçaient ce dimanche à Lille où elles se sont imposées 1-0 grâce à une Ithaisa Vinoly impériale dans les buts et un but tardif d'Héloïse Mansuy. Un cinquième succès consécutif en championnat qui permet à l'ASSE de conforter sa place de leader et de semer la concurrence !Après leur victoire convaincante face à Auxerre (2-0), les Vertes faisaient face dans le Nord à un concurrent sérieux dans la course à la montée et Yannick Chandioux devait en plus composer sans Rachel Corboz et Agathe Felden, absentes surprises de dernière minute.Sur la pelouse synthétique du LOSC, ce qui avait obligé les Stéphanoises à s'entraîner toute la semaine sur une surface identique à Saint-Priest-en-Jarez, les Vertes ne connaissaient pourtant pas l'entame espérée, les Lilloises imposant rapidement leur rythme et se montrant particulièrement agressives dans les duels. Dès la 9e minute, la meilleure buteuse de D2, Coralie Monguillon se présentait face à Ithaisa Vinoly, mais la gardienne espagnole remportait son face-à-face et préservait les cages stéphanoises.Le LOSC continuait de pousser et Wissem Bouzid voyait ensuite une nouvelle tentative dangereuse contrée in extremis avant que Chloé Neller ne trouve le poteau à quelques minutes de la pause. Côté stéphanois, seule Sarah Cambot parvenait à répondre sur une belle reprise de volée qui frôlait le montant adverse. A la pause, les Vertes pouvaient s'estimer heureuses de ne pas être menées.Mais au retour des vestiaires, les Amazones affichaient de meilleures intentions et se montraient davantage entreprenantes, même si les Lilloises continuaient de poser de sérieux problèmes à l'arrière-garde verte. À l'heure de jeu, un énorme cafouillage dans la surface stéphanoise faisait même passer un sérieux frisson dans le camp forézien avant de finir en corner.Les minutes défilaient et les joueuses de Yannick Chandioux peinaient toujours à prendre véritablement le contrôle de la rencontre. À la 74e minute, Coralie Monguillon se heurtait encore à une Ithaisa Vinoly exceptionnelle. La portière espagnole réalisait une parade déterminante et permettait aux siennes de rester dans le match.Et ce ne fut pas en vain car à 10 minutes de la fin du match, sur un coup de pied arrêté, un ballon traînait dans les cinq mètres cinquante nordistes et Héloïse Mansuy surgissait pour le pousser au fond des filets. Un avantage aussi précieux qu'inattendu (0-1, 80e) !Les dernières minutes devenaient alors particulièrement tendues, les Lilloises vexées tentant de revenir au score par tous les moyens possibles, faisant progressivement monter la nervosité entre les deux bancs.Sans surprise, la tension dégénéra et entraîna une pluie de cartons à la 90e minute, l'entraîneur lillois Mathieu Robail et la buteuse Coralie Monguillon étant ainsi expulsés mais malgré cette fin de rencontre particulièrement agitée, les Vertes ne lâchaient rien et conservaient leur avantage jusqu'au coup de sifflet final.Un précieux succès, acquis dans la douleur, qui permettait aux Amazones d'enchaîner une cinquième victoire consécutive en championnat et de prolonger leur invincibilité.Yannick Chandioux: "On retiendra avant tout la victoire. Et celle-ci, on la doit en grande partie à notre gardienne, Ithaïsa. Elle nous a maintenues dans le match pendant longtemps, alors que nous sommes passées à côté de notre entame, quasiment dès les premières minutes. Nous avons été moins agressives que les Lilloises, qui ont pleinement joué sur cet aspect et nous ont mises en difficulté (...) Ne pas avoir encaissé de but dans cette rencontre relève presque du petit hold-up. (...) Parfois, les saisons réussies se construisent aussi dans ce genre de matchs, que l'on parvient à gagner alors que peu de monde nous voyait l'emporter".Avec 17 points au compteur, les Stéphanoises conservent bien sûr leur fauteuil de leader avec deux longueurs d'avance sur Le Mans et trois sur Roubaix. Nintendo Doesn’t Need To Bring Its Games To Other Platforms, Former President Says

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Former Nintendo of America president Doug Bowser says that the company doesn’t currently “feel the need” to bring its games to other consoles.

As it makes clear at pretty much every opportunity, Nintendo marches to the beat of its own drum more than any other company in gaming. It has its own faults, sure, but while PlayStation and Xbox chase trends like live-services and put most of their time and money into trying to push graphics even further, Nintendo is mostly content just making fun games.

Unlike PlayStation with its temporary foray into porting games to PC and Xbox and its controversial, since walked back third-party push, Nintendo notably likes to keep its franchises solely on its own hardware. Even with console exclusives changing so much this console generation, it sounds like Nintendo won't be steering away from that approach any time soon.

As pointed out by My Nintendo News, GamesBeat recently assembled some notable industry veterans to talk about the current state of gaming. One of the members of the panel was none other than former Nintendo of America president Doug Bowser, who shed some light on the company’s approach to releasing its software and games.

We finally got a chance to play The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake and it's still one of the best games ever made.

Bowser said that the conversation about creating consoles and games has always been “different” for Nintendo since both teams work together to “optimise the build” at the “lowest costs”. The former president pointed out that there’s a “huge gap” in the cost of development at Nintendo and every other studio.

That approach is something that has “always worked” for the company, with Bowser adding that it’s focused on creating a “Nintendo universe”. Interestingly, Bowser said that Nintendo doesn’t “feel the need” to “take it beyond the platform”, suggesting that Nintendo simply isn’t interested in bringing its games to other consoles.

The only real outlier to that claim is Nintendo’s mobile outings like Super Mario Run and Animal Crossing: Pocket Camp. As Mario Kart World Tour proved, though, that's not really a long-term focus.

Of course, this isn’t exactly a revelation, as Nintendo has always been known for keeping its exclusives, well, exclusive and being incredibly protective of its IP. Still, with Xbox and PlayStation both experimenting with wider releases, it’s nice to know that one console leader is sticking with its own audience.

Man United’s unique comeback leaves no one knowing how to feel

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Manchester United 4-4 Aston Villa: Michael Carrick was applauded off as his players rescued an extraordinary draw, even though his side remained 18th in the Premier League and on an eight-game winless run

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

As the Manchester United players who had been told they were not fit to wear the shirt, in perhaps the earliest ever airing of that chant in footballing history, celebrated a second ridiculous comeback in five days, the Stretford End launched into a different chorus.

They had told the team they were unworthy of representing them after a mere five minutes, had jeered in damning fashion when the dreadful Diogo Dalot was substituted, had been vitriolic about the Glazer family, Sir Jim Ratcliffe and Jason Wilcox. But United left the pitch in the relegation zone while their supporters serenaded Michael Carrick.

“It’s hard to believe it’s not Scholes,” they sang. In one respect, it was hard to believe it was not Sir Alex Ferguson, plucking results from nowhere. In another, it was hard to believe this was not the prelude to a sacking. It may still be.

But Carrick won’t quit. “That’s not going to happen,” he said. “I am quite clear what direction the club needs to go in and we need to win games.” They have only won once in 11 games, one point in three in the Premier League but Carrick is starting to specialise in escapology. Bournemouth had seemed the end for him, Roma looked it when his side were 2-0 down. Aston Villa seemed his valedictory game twice, before two two-goal comebacks.

“Making it hard for ourselves,” said Carrick. That was putting it mildly. Yet his side have twice delivered a show of spirit to salvage something from the brink of humiliation. Ferguson’s most famous comeback was delivered by two substitutes, Teddy Sheringham and Ole Gunnar Solskjaer. If Carrick has a lifeline now, it came from two replacements, scorers in Fergie Time, when it seemed time had run out for the latest United manager.

First Patrick Dorgu set up Joshua Zirkzee to halve the deficit. Then Dorgu headed in Bruno Fernandes’ 96th-minute corner. “In extra time conceding two goals is painful,” said Unai Emery. “Today we have lost two points.” Emery headed off for the tunnel when Dorgu scored, a picture of frustration. “I was angry with the situation, not with the players,” he said. A few minutes earlier, the Villa supporters had been jubilant, taunting their former midfielder Youri Tielemans with a chorus of: “Youri, what’s the score?”

Few, Emery included, foresaw the eventual answer. Villa were on course for their biggest victory at Old Trafford since 1920; then, suddenly, they did not have a win at all. This was confusing and complicated, crazy entertainment. United showed a self-destructive streak at the start of each half and reacted to spread the kind of chaos that put a smile back on their faces.

“I am disappointed we didn’t win the game,” claimed Carrick. United were both awful and exciting. They have character, if not competence at the back, where they did an impression of a particularly hungover pub team. A team with a series of historic lows have a pan-European level of porousness. As Villa scored four, no team in Europe’s five major leagues has conceded more goals in all competitions than United. Defensively, they are disastrous, disorganised, struggling at set-pieces, forever caught out by the counter-attack. Dalot is in hideous form and it got worse. Carrick erred by not swapping him with Noussair Mazraoui at half-time. What followed was all too predictable as, unable to contain the outstanding Alysson, Dalot fouled him to concede a penalty. Yet when Dalot eventually did go off, he was replaced by a rescuer, in the shape of the fit-again Dorgu.

Before his introduction, United had looked a shambles as they made a shocking start, and then another. They twice responded to Carrick’s team talks by capitulating, conceding two goals in four minutes at the start of the match and two in eight after the break.

Two goals behind after 18 minutes to Roma, United somehow surpassed that. This was the earliest they had ever been 2-0 down in a Premier League game when Villa doubled their lead, with Senne Lammens at fault for both.

Dalot should share the blame for Alyson’s first-minute opener, allowing the winger too much room to shoot. He beat the goalkeeper at his near post. When Lammens only pushed another Alysson shot straight to Nicolas Jackson, the summer signing slotted in his eighth goal in his last nine games.

It was, ridiculously, the fifth consecutive game in which United went 2-0 down. Briefly, they hinted at a second consecutive 3-2 win, only for something still more outlandish to unfold. The comeback against Roma began with a penalty won by Matheus Cunha and scored by Fernandes. Five days later, Boubacar Kamara fouled the Brazilian and the United captain sent Zion Suzuki the wrong way. Déjà vu.

As United were forced into a pattern of frenetic attacking, Cunha tried to power another fightback. There were echoes of Tuesday night, waves of attack. Cunha, Fernandes and Amad Diallo all came close. Suzuki made fine saves from Cunha and Amad. Tielemans swept in his first United goal from Mazraoui’s cutback.

After the interval, Villa felt fired up again. United emerged dopily. Carrick left Dalot up against the outstanding Alysson and tripped him. Either the winger was unstoppable or United made him look so but John McGinn, who missed his penalty against Fulham last week, scored this.

Then Lammens made his save of the game, sparing Leny Yoro an own goal but in vain. Jackson scuffed his shot but Leon Goretzka finished. His first Villa goal, it seemed, would finish off Carrick and United. Somehow, extraordinarily, it did not. They struck back in added time. His reign may be in added time, too.

Who gets to block your Kalshi bets? Washington just escalated the fight

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

US derivatives exchanges face a comply-or-explain demand as separate court holdings keep Kalshi's nationwide access unresolved.

The Commodity Futures Trading Commission's November 5 demand gives US derivatives exchanges 30 days to remove domestic geographic restrictions that violate its impartial-access rules or explain why compliance is not feasible. That puts fresh federal pressure on the geographic controls the Sixth Circuit said could coexist with prediction market Kalshi's federal obligations.

The conflict centers on geofencing: using a trader's location to determine which markets they can access. The CFTC now says otherwise eligible US participants generally should not be excluded by geography. In September, the Sixth Circuit reasoned that exchanges could use geographic limits while meeting federal obligations.

The instruction applies to designated contract markets, or DCMs. The same release says they should ensure intermediaries accessing their markets comply within 30 days, or explain why compliance is not feasible.

The CFTC's interpretation expects exchanges to examine access criteria, exchange rules and intermediary arrangements.

The Commission says it will consider each case's facts and circumstances before potential action. The 30-day demand comes from the dated release; the accompanying PDF remains subject to pre-publication technical corrections and retains an unfilled effective-publication-date placeholder.

The CFTC argues that excluding eligible traders can fragment liquidity and weaken price discovery. Its guidance highlights election markets where local participants may contribute useful information, presenting these as potential costs of geographic exclusion.

In its September 25 preliminary-injunction opinion, the Sixth Circuit held that Kalshi had not shown its sports-event contracts met the relevant statutory definition of a swap, the classification behind its claim to exclusive CFTC oversight. It read that definition as requiring an inherent financial, economic or commercial consequence, rather than the downstream effects of a sports result.

The panel then reached an alternative holding: even assuming the contracts were swaps, the Commodity Exchange Act did not expressly or impliedly preempt Ohio and Tennessee gambling laws.

Geofencing appeared within that second analysis. Rejecting Kalshi's argument that compliance with both regimes was impossible, the judges read impartial access as access to whatever markets an exchange offers. They did not read it as requiring every event contract to be available in every state, and pointed to geographic controls used by other companies.

The new CFTC position directly challenges that regulatory premise. But changing the interpretation of an access rule does not, by itself, undo the court's swap-classification decision or the rest of its preemption analysis.

The CFTC challenges the access rationale; its interpretation does not reverse the separate swap and preemption holdings.

The release covers DCM restrictions that violate impartial access and permits an infeasibility explanation. The diagrams are schematics of legal readings, not a current Kalshi access map or a statement about any mandate or stay.

The Sixth Circuit affirmed the denial of Kalshi's Ohio preliminary injunction, vacated its Tennessee injunction and remanded. That ruling was not itself an instruction to geoblock users in either state.

A separate CFTC proposal published October 19 would expressly include sports, political, cultural and weather event contracts in the swap definition. Comments are due November 18.

The geographic limits reach crypto distribution. Crypto wallet Phantom's availability table lists Kalshi prediction markets in the US except Michigan, Nevada and Washington. Its October 28 integration announcement says restrictions vary by market category, and that Kalshi approves and holds trading accounts and executes trades submitted through the wallet.

Court obligations can be narrower than a state boundary. A November 4 federal order in California enjoined Kalshi Inc. and KalshiEX from offering sports contracts on the plaintiff Tribes' Indian lands. It requires boundary maps by November 13 and a compliance status report within 21 days. Its scope is those lands, rather than all of California.

Separately, Kalshi's response to New Jersey's Supreme Court petition is due December 7. The Court's docket records the October 30 extension. That petition comes from the Third Circuit and could give the justices a route to address the federal-state dispute if they grant review. The docket shows no grant.

The immediate operational test is the exchanges' response to the 30-day demand: which barriers they remove and which they explain to the Division of Market Oversight.

Coordinated disturbances haunt grids worldwide. Here’s how researchers solved the mystery.

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

For years, mysterious electrical anomalies have been flummoxing grid managers around the world. Every 60 seconds, the frequency on the grid would drop a tiny bit.

Even weirder: The sags happened at the exact same time on power grids across countries and continents.

It was a mystery that Dirk Witthaut, a researcher at Forschungszentrum Jülich in Germany, couldn’t pass up.

Germany’s largest transmission system operator, Tennet Germany, brought the puzzle to Witthaut, and it took years of investigative and modeling work to determine the cause. From Europe to Australia, the U.S. to Malaysia, the same pattern shows up in the data.

Such frequency drops aren’t unheard of, but they are troubling. And the regularity with which they have been occurring can make the phenomenon feel even more ominous.

The grid’s orderly operation relies on the frequency being kept within a certain range. If the oscillations get get too large, or if grid operators don’t know the cause and respond improperly, they can lead to blackouts. Even if the sags don’t cause blackouts, they can damage equipment in power plants if they go unchecked, especially in hydropower turbines.

Hydropower plants have historically been a main driver of so-called ultra-low frequency oscillations (UFLOs). Every turbine vibrates, and if those vibrations occur with a certain regularity, known as the resonant frequency, they can magnify, sometimes to dangerous levels. Think of a wine glass breaking before an opera singer’s mighty pipes — that’s resonance.

So Witthaut and his team started investigating hydropower, the most obvious potential culprit. They used a computer model of a hydropower plant control system on the Scandinavian grid to test the hypothesis.

While they managed to find conditions that could generate a resonance that peaked once per minute, they couldn’t explain why it persisted for so long. Typically, resonances in mechanical systems will come and go, and so will the accompanying signal on the electrical grid. But this frequency drop kept happening minute after minute after minute. Every 60 seconds, without fail.

It then dawned on the team that anything operating with such clockwork required, well, a clock. How else could they explain the uncanny timing?

To that hypothesis, the researchers started close to home, installing more than 80 smart power quality meters around the Jülich campus. The research site is outfitted with medium- and low-voltage distribution lines, transformers, solar panels, and battery energy storage systems that serve as uninterruptible power supplies (UPS). It also has a high-performance computer system as well as the usual office computers powered by much smaller UPS devices. The power quality meters sampled a range of attributes, including frequency, voltage, current, and power, once every three seconds.

Before long, the research team had its answer. The measurements recorded on the transformer attached to the battery energy storage system peaked every minute, just like on the bigger power grids.

It turns out that many stationary batteries, from data center UPSes to large grid-scale batteries, check their state-of-charge every minute. Since these devices are internet-connected, their clocks are in near perfect synchronicity. Every time one checks its charge, the disturbance is imperceptible on the wide grid. But when they all do it together, that disruption becomes apparent.

To find evidence that UPSes were the main culprit on the grid, Witthaut and his team turned to data provided by grid operators. They found their answer in Ireland.

There, data centers have grown to consume a large fraction of the country’s electricity. By 2022, data centers represented 18% of the load on Ireland’s grid, and each facility is served by a fleet of UPSes. Sure enough, between 2016 and 2021, the minute-by-minute frequency drop on the Irish grid quadrupled from 3 milli-Hertz (mHz) in 2016 to 12 mHz in 2021. More recently, some drops have exceeded 20 mHz.

Ireland’s 50 Hz grid is built to withstand swings of plus or minus 200 mHz, though the grid operator would rather limit the swings to 100 mHz. In that range, 20 mHz isn’t a lot, but data centers aren’t the only loads on the grid. If other loads also cause the frequency to sag, the extra bump from every UPS checking its state-of-charge could push the grid toward blackout conditions.

The problem is entirely preventable. Rather than check state-of-charge exactly on the minute, Witthaus and team recommend that UPS and grid-scale battery manufacturers add some randomization to the schedule.

While UPSes are driving the problem today, there’s a risk that the phenomenon could persist. The researchers also tested IoT devices. In devices as diverse as a smart oven and Raspberry Pi clusters, they were able to detect periodic power fluctuations. The fix is also a simple software patch, but many IoT devices go unpatched. A recent study found that less than 3% of IoT devices are running the latest firmware.

Regulators may soon have to step in to demand changes, but they can only address their corner of the grid. Given the global nature of the issue, it’ll ultimately require global coordination. The mystery may be solved, but the problem could remain for years to come.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Nvidia has announced that it is opening an investigation into CEO Jensen Huang and his relationship with Megaspeed executive Alice Huang, who was reportedly recruited by China’s Ministry of State Security to spy on the company. A statement posted by the board of directors reads:

The NVIDIA Board is initiating an independent investigation in light of recent reporting that founder and CEO Jensen Huang has been the target of a foreign intelligence effort. A committee of independent directors, assisted by outside independent counsel, will oversee the investigation.

The Board prioritizes U.S. national and economic security, which is paramount to the interests of NVIDIA and its shareholders. While the Board has no reason to believe that either national security or NVIDIA secrets were compromised, the Board’s inquiry will examine all available information. Jensen fully supports the review and has committed to cooperating with it.

The Board has confidence in Jensen and his leadership team and will provide further updates as appropriate.

The New York Times reported last month that Huang had developed a “close relationship” with the Megaspeed executive, which he later admitted was romantic in nature in a memo to the company. US officials believe she was feeding information directly back to Beijing.

Megaspeed is a customer of Nvidia and is the subject of a Commerce Department investigation into whether it was smuggling AI chips to China. The company’s AI chips are viewed as crucial to the US maintaining technological supremacy. Both the Biden and Trump administrations have restricted their sale to China. President Trump has loosened those restrictions but still bars sales of Nvidia’s most powerful chips to Chinese firms, and the Commerce Department must approve individual firms to handle the chips.

Just as importantly, Jensen Huang has gotten quite cozy with Donald Trump, and there is concern about what insights into the administration’s deliberations around AI he may have been privy to. And whether any of that information may have been shared with Alice Huang, and eventually found its way to Beijing.

Michael Carrick clashes with Sky Sports reporter in spiky interview over his future: Man United boss expects to continue despite club being in relegation zone after 4-4 draw with Villa

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

By CHRIS WHEELER, NORTHERN SPORTS WRITER and LUKE AUGUSTUS, ASSISTANT SPORTS EDITOR

Published: 15:17 EDT, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 16:08 EDT, 8 November 2026

Michael Carrick insists he won't quit as Manchester United boss despite having to see his team spend the international break in the relegation zone.

United staged another incredible fightback to snatch a 4-4 draw with Aston Villa at Old Trafford after trailing by two goals heading into injury-time.

But it wasn't enough to spare the club from the humiliation of being in the bottom three for the next two weeks – the longest United have spent there since 1986.

The manner of the performance and result will only heap further pressure on Carrick and when the future of his own position was put to him by Sky Sports reporter Patrick Davison, the United boss took exception to the question in a spiky interview.

'What do you expect to happen over the next few weeks? You've been asked time and again about your future? I know you have said that you walking away was the best for the club, you'd probably do it,' Davison asked Carrick.

'It's not going to happen, no,' Carrick replied.

"That's not going to happen" 🗣️Michael Carrick responds to the notion that he could resign as Manchester United head coach 🔴 pic.twitter.com/q1fBKSXL7l

Michael Carrick was involved in a spiky interview with Sky Sports about his Man United future

Patrick Dorgu (centre) scored a late equaliser as troubled Man United drew 4-4 vs Aston Villa

Despite Sunday's draw, Carrick's position as United head coach looks increasingly doubtful

With United chiefs Omar Berrada and Jason Wilcox in the stands, Davison pushed further asking: 'What do you expect to happen? Clearly there's going to be conversation?'

This prompted Carrick to quizzically state: 'Is there?' as the pair engaged in a back-and-forth on the subject.

Eventually Carrick replied: 'You know the situation, we haven't won enough games. But I expect to move forward with things we've been working on, showing a lot of the good from today tighten up a few mistakes and win some football matches.'

United's start of one win in 10 games is still their worst since Alex Ferguson replaced Ron Atkinson 40 years ago this week. They conceded twice at the start of each half against Villa, and have now let in 30 goals in all competitions – the joint highest in Europe's top five leagues.

'We're making it hard for ourselves. Mistakes that the boys know shouldn't happen,' added Carrick. 'Two goals again put us on the back foot. We beat ourselves a little bit with the goals that we're giving away in tight clusters. It's hard enough anyway, but we're making it a lot more difficult than it should be.

'I thought we were the better team. We never gave up and never stopped believing. I'm disappointed that we still haven't won the game, to be truthful.'

The United fans kept behind Carrick and his team while once again targeting the club's owners, chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox.

However, there were loud cheers when Diogo Dalot was replaced in the 66th minute. It was reminiscent of the treatment of Joshua Zirkzee by the home supporters when he was substituted against Newcastle in December 2024, and the Dutchman made a point of giving Dalot a reassuring hug in the dugout.

'We've got to be careful with that,' said Carrick. 'I understand the frustration and the disappointment of the supporters, so I get it. We're all frustrated, we're all disappointed, and the players are as much as anyone, I guarantee you that.

'Diogo gives absolutely everything. He's played in different positions, he's played left-back, he's played really well at times.

'He gave a penalty away today, he knows that. Maybe he can do a little bit better. He's made a mistake, but he cares an awful lot and he's giving everything.'

Dalot's replacement Patrick Dorgu led the fightback, five days after United had recovered from two goals down to beat Roma 3-2, as he crossed for Zirkzee to pull a goal back and then headed the equaliser in the 96th minute.

'I don't feel happy,' said Dorgu. 'We are in a bad situation and we need to fight together to get out of it. A draw is better than a loss, but everyone needs to look at themselves and ask if they are doing enough. We need to fight for the manager, for the club and get out of this together.

'Of course, it hurts (to be in relegation zone). There are a lot of games and we need everyone to come back sharp from international break and start getting wins.'

Teammate Matheus Cunha added: 'We are so disappointed. Before anything else we need to look inside to us – the players, the staff. It's a huge club and to feel something like this (being in the relegation zone) is not normal.'

It's not the first time Carrick has engaged in an interesting post-match interview this season. In August, he snapped at a reporter's 'ridiculous' opening question after Manchester United's shock defeat at Hull City on the opening day of the season.

Carrick was affronted when asked if his players had lost against the newly promoted club due to a lack of 'passion'.

United's Premier League season got off to a nightmare start as goals from Semi Ajayi and Nobel Mendy - both stemming from set-pieces - put them to the sword in Yorkshire.

Carrick was interviewed afterwards by Viaplay broadcaster Jan Age Fjortoft, the Norwegian who played for Swindon, Middlesbrough, Sheffield United and Barnsley in the 1990s.

“Come on, that’s ridiculous.”Michael Carrick wasn’t happy when asked if there was a lack of passion from #mufc in their first half vs Hull.via @ViaplayFotball pic.twitter.com/zyca5r5iSr

In August, Carrick said it was 'ridiculous' to suggest United lacked passion after losing to Hull

He was vexed by the question posed by Viaplay reporter and former play Jan Age Fjortoft

'Would you accept that there was a lack of passion in the first half?' asked Fjortoft.

'Oh come on, that's ridiculous,' Carrick hit back.

'That's not how you would assess it?' asked Fjortoft.

Carrick replied: 'Come on, you played football, you understand, that's a ridiculous thing to say. Just because you got beat doesn't mean you don't care about it.'

Fjortoft said: 'I'm not saying that you didn't care about it. If you look at it, they came with passion, that's what I mean. I understand that you care about it, but would you accept that they took you on that front, so to say?'

'No, no. Their two goals came from set plays. Other than that, we had opportunities,' Carrick said. 'That was where the game was. I don't think it was passion or anything to do with that. I think it was just their moment that made the difference in the game.'

Fjortoft said: 'Is it these duels? Is that what you mean? That these two duels were the goals? Because they had chances. There were consequences there from the game that you could see that they would get at you.'

'It's a game of football, it's a Premier League game,' Carrick replied.

'It's their first game at home, and we've seen it many times before. You know, I said it before the game because it was always going to be a tough game.

'And set-plays, yeah, that's it. We've been decent over a period of time at dealing with them, and today we didn't.

'We can look into that, we can dissect it. We certainly won't get carried away with the positives or the negatives. We never do. So I think we can stay calm and understand what we need to get better at.'

After the international break though United face the ultimate litmus test when they travel to bitter rivals Liverpool at Anfield in the Premier League on November 22.

Gary Neville names four players who will ‘get Man Utd relegated’ after Aston Villa draw

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Gary Neville has slammed Manchester United’s failure to sign any new defenders last summer and says the back-four that finished the remarkable 4-4 draw with Aston Villa will ‘get the club relegated’.

The Premier League giants go into the November international break sitting 18th in the table after Villa twice blew two-goal leads to draw at Old Trafford.

United found themselves 2-0 down inside five minutes as Alysson and Nicolas Jackson took advantage of the hosts’ fragile defence.

Michael Carrick’s side were level at half-time thanks to Bruno Fernandes’ penalty and Youri Tielemans’ 44th-minute strike against the club he left last summer

Sign up to our daily football newsletter, pick your team and get the latest news sent directly to your inbox every morning.

Josh McGinn restored Aston Villa’s lead with a penalty straight after the interval and the visitors looked on course for a big win when Leon Goretzka scored a fourth.

But Manchester United scored twice in injury-time – substitutes Joshua Zirkzee and Patrick Dorgu with the goals – to salvage a point and avoid sitting joint-bottom during the international break.

Despite two comebacks, United’s current position is their lowest at this stage of a season since 1986, while their eight-game winless league run has only happened once before.

Manchester United finished the Aston Villa thriller with a back-four of Noussair Mazraoui, Leny Yoro, Ayden Heaven and Patrick Dorgu, and Neville said that line-up would struggle to avoid relegation to the Championship.

‘The reason Manchester United have conceded four goals is because they haven’t invested in the defence in the transfer window,’ he said on NBC Sports.

Use AI to go deeper into the stories you care about – powered by Metro and trusted publications.

‘Every one knew they had to do that, they needed defenders. The current ones are making mistake after mistake after mistake.

‘The defenders that finished that game will get you relegated. Watch the defensive errors… we all screamed in the transfer window that they have to bring some defenders in.

‘They had to bring the transfer budget forward and bring defenders in.’

Gary’s brother Phil, who also enjoyed incredible success at Manchester United as a player, added: ‘The back-four that finished the game – Mazraoui, Yoro, Heaven and Dorgu – that gets you relegated.

"Should be banging heads!" 😡Roy Keane discusses how Manchester United can improve their form pic.twitter.com/r7JZzj62LD

‘That back-four gets you the sack as a manager and that back-four cannot keep United, I don’t think, in the Premier League.

‘That back-four gets you relegated, it’s poor. It’s not good enough to play in the Premier League.’

Despite Manchester United’s grim position in the Premier League, Gary Neville doubts a change in the dugout would improve the situation in the long-term.

‘You can paper over the cracks and get a new manager bounce,’ he added. ‘When the new manager comes in, everybody’s happy for a couple of months and all of a sudden, six months later, it happens again.

‘You can keep changing the manager and thinking that’s going to be the answer. But this has been proven now over eight managers in 12 years, that’s not the answer.

‘Michael could leave, Michael might stay, we don’t know, the ownership don’t have a clue what to do.

‘It’s rinse and repeat. Amorim, Ten Hag, Ole Gunnar Solskjaer, Carrick, Jose Mourinho, Louis van Gaal, David Moyes, Ralf Rangnick – some of them are world class managers who have had success, there are a lot of promising young managers there, ex players who know the club as well.

‘None of it has worked because if you recruit badly, if you’ve got poor performance department, poor recruitment department, you’ve not got leadership that is strong right through the spine of the football club you will fail.

‘Every organisation in the world will fail if you’ve not got an aligned leadership from top to bottom – your board and your ownership right the way through to the person who is stood on the pitch trying to play football.

‘There are real issues within that football club, you’ve got people doing those jobs who have never done those jobs before in senior positions.’

Denis Villeneuve Praises Creative Process Over AI Prompts and Stops Short of Naming New Bond at LACMA Art+Film Gala

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

The stars were out Saturday night at the milestone 15th annual fundraiser, presented once again by Gucci and featuring a night-ending performance by global superstar Rosé.

Museums are many things: cultural institutions that serve as sources of inspiration, exhibitions of creativity, reflections of humanity, bridges to the past and freeways to the future. For Denis Villeneuve, museums have “always been super food.”

“Every time I walk into one, I feel like a vampire sucking the energy from the walls. And, one way or another, it ends up in one of my films,” explained the blockbuster director while standing at a podium Saturday night on the grounds of one of his favorites, LACMA in Los Angeles.

Museums, especially this one, can also just be a great place for a glamorous party. Every November, LACMA transforms its grounds into a nest for A-listers from many industries for its marquee fundraiser, Art+Film, which honors a chosen one from each discipline. Representing cinema this year, honoree Villeneuve masterfully paid respects to both as he accepted his prize following a tribute from casual acquaintance and devoted admirer Nicole Kidman, who flew in from Australia specifically to say some nice things on stage. (Villeneuve returned the favor by praising her risk-taking across decades and calling her work in Jonathan Glazer’s Birth, “one of the most extraordinary performances I have ever seen on screen.”) Related Stories Lifestyle GQ Expands Men of the Year Footprint in L.A. Lifestyle Best Celebrity Halloween Costumes 2026: Paris Hilton as Britney Spears, Lisa Rinna as Kylie Jenner and More

“When a museum, a temple for artists, welcomes a filmmaker and puts the spotlight on the cinema, it means a great deal for me, especially this museum because LACMA has already given me so much,” explained Villeneuve, honored on the eve of the anticipated release of Dune: Part Three on Dec. 18. “As an example, I was preparing Arrival and I didn’t yet know how to direct the film. I was looking for guidance. Then I walked into the James Turrell exhibition here in LACMA [on view May 26, 2013-April 6, 2014]. I watched people standing in a room in front of a bridge, covered in light, in silence and in awe. Then I thought, OK, I know how to direct the film now. I know how to direct Arrival. And Arrival, in many ways, is an homage to the afternoon spent at LACMA.”

Villeneuve delivered the remarks in a tented pavilion that hosted more than 600 guests, including movie stars, actors, moguls, models, artists, philanthropists, fashion designers and more. Soaking in the scene were his Dune stars Timothée Chalamet (with Kylie Jenner) and Anya Taylor Joy, Viola Davis, Kim Kardashian, Al Pacino, Andrew Garfield with Monica Barbaro, Sarah Paulson, Curry Barker, Daniel Scheinert, Jerry Bruckheimer, Larry Gagosian, Alix Earle, Julia Garner, Phil Lord, Alex Consani, Paris Hilton, Odessa A’zion, Sienna Miller, Spike Jonze, Tyriq Withers, Emma Watson, Parker Posey, CAA’s Joel Lubin and Franklin Latt, artists Alex Israel, Alex Prager, Barbara Kruger, Cathy Opie, Ed Ruscha, Honor Titus, Delfin and Kohshin Finley, Lauren Halsey, Mark Bradford, Charles Gaines and many more. (See THR’s red carpet gallery for more names here.) Representing Gucci, back for another year as presenting sponsor, were creative director Demna and CEO Francesca Bellettini.

Some boldfaced names not in the building still got shout-outs from the podium. Villeneuve recalled how, as a pre-teen growing up in Quebec, Canada, all of his pals were reading about hockey while he was obsessed with Steven Spielberg. “He became my hero,” he said. “Steven is the reason I’m standing here today, and I’m lucky enough now to be able to call him a friend. And then came James Cameron. These are titans.” He also name-checked fellow auteurs Alfonso Cuarón, Alejandro González Iñárritu and Chloé Zhao, “three fellow artists pushing the boundaries of cinematic expression in the most original ways.”

Kidman, a unique choice to offer a tribute to Villeneuve because she admitted she’s “never really met Denis” and they’ve never worked together. “I’ve met him on some red carpets and sort of waved at him, and at one time I said hello in a crowded room at an Armani store. It is L.A.,” she noted. “And yet somehow I feel like I know the deepest parts of him because that is what extraordinary filmmakers do. They let you in. They reveal themselves to us through their work. They invite you into their imaginations, into their dreams, into their questions, and into their fears. I saw in Incendies, Prisoners, Sicario, Blade Runner, Arrival, and of course the Dune movies, and I feel like that transcends anything that needs an introduction because his films explore the great mysteries of our existence.”

Kidman said when she was asked to take the stage, it was an immediate yes even if it required a long-haul flight. “I just love his movies, as I’m sure so many people in this room do. I really love them though, because when they asked me to present this award, I knew I was going to be in Australia and I went, ‘Absolutely. I will fly 15 hours to be here to be able to give him this award.’ And the reason for that is because I want to say thank you to Denis. Thank you because you make big movies, you make extraordinary movies, you make important movies and you make sympathetic movies. They are vast, they’re mysterious, they’re sometimes terrifying, but they are always profoundly human.”

In his acceptance speech, Villeneuve revealed a little about his creative expression, too, and used it as a lead-in to confront the existential crisis imposed by artificial intelligence. “At the heart of every one of my films, there’s a question I need to answer. The audience may see it or they may not, and that is fine. For me, each film is a statement to the unknown to try and find that answer. Perhaps those questions are more urgent now than ever,” he explained. “We are at the crossroad, asking what defines a human being and what human expression is worth? Every parent on Earth is a little worried about the world of our children will inherit. That question is at the heart of Dune: Part Three with Paul and Chani fighting to give their children a better world than the one they were given.”

He continued: “For a filmmaker today, it is very real. I have a choice. I can create something with a prompt or I can go into the desert with actors, with a crew and go on a real adventure where I learn about life and become better at my craft. Those are two different things. Well, I know there’s producers in the room and they will say that one is more expensive than the other. More important than the result is the process. The process is how the human experience acquires its meaning. In this moment in time, it is more important than ever that human beings keep making art together. That is the heritage I want to give to my children.”

Speaking of heritage, Villeneuve will next take the reins of the iconic James Bond franchise as his next directing project, which is currently in the late stages of a hotly followed casting process. To close his speech, Villeneuve played into the headlines with a little mic drop moment: “To express my gratitude with the blessing of my new friends at Amazon, I will reveal to you tonight who will be the next James Bond. The new James Bond is… and I will stop right now because Amy Pascal is about to have a heart attack. Thank you very much.”

The fake-out drew plenty of laughs. In lieu of the naming of a new 007, there was some other breaking news shared from the stage as longtime co-chair Eva Chow, who has presided over the gala since its inception alongside Leonardo DiCaprio, announced that she would be stepping down after the milestone 15th installment Saturday. LACMA chief Michael Govan telegraphed that something major was coming as he saved a generous portion of his introductory comments for Chow by praising her as the super connector and glamorous glue that helped solidify the event and make it such a fundraising force for the institution. (Govan also announced that Saturday’s event, the first to be held following the opening of the landmark David Geffen Galleries, raised a record-breaking nearly $7 million, “our best year yet.”)

“Your love of art comes from your own practice. As a young girl, you were a bit of a watercolor painting prodigy, so art was in your DNA from the beginning. And of course you went on to be a fashion designer and your art continues to be strong, especially in bringing creative worlds together. As a trustee of LACMA who is now living in LA and Seoul, you perfectly epitomize our local and global reach,” Govan said. “You’ve added so much to our vision of connecting many fields, many continents, many worlds, and you do it all with a sense of style and beauty that is remarkable.”

Govan then welcomed Chow to the stage to deliver remarks, which she always does during Art+Film. But this time, she took a brief walk down memory lane before making her announcement. She recalled how “exciting, challenging and ambitious” the early days were when they were getting the fundraiser off the ground. She called in favors to people like Stevie Wonder, who agreed to be the musical performer at the inaugural event, which honored Clint Eastwood and John Baldessari.

“That first evening set the tone for everything that followed,” she said. “It is difficult to put 15 years of memories, friendship, experiences into a few words. This gala has been such an important part of my life. To have started with an idea and to see it grow into one of Los Angeles’ most celebrated traditions has been one of great privileges of my life. I leave this chapter with tremendous pride, wonderful memories, and above all gratitude. I hope this gala will continue to grow and inspire future generations, bringing together the world of art and film for many years to come. Tonight, let’s celebrate our honorees and the creativity, the friendships and everything we have built together.”

The night’s other honoree, representing the art world, was Vija Celmins, who charmed the crowd with a brief yet endearing acceptance speech and in a tribute video produced by LACMA Productions, seen below. After thanking Govan and her art dealer Matthew Marks, Celmins quipped, “It’s a pleasure to be here. You guys are just crazy party people. It’s really a revelation for me.”

Global pop superstar Rosé closed the show with a four-song set that included her smash hit “APT,” “Number One Girl” (as requested by Chow), the Killers cover “Read My Mind” and her new single “new trick.” She wrapped at 10:30 p.m., and many of the guests headed straight for the valet and off into the night for the next stop at an invite-only after party at Chow’s house. Maybe Celmins was right.

Why Kylie Jenner and Timothée Chalamet Didn’t Pose Together on the LACMA Art+Film Gala Red Carpet

Jenner arrived solo, posing for photographers in a fun Ashi Studio gown, and Chalamet skipped the red carpet altogether. He was snapped inside the event on his own.

Nvidia investigates CEO Jensen Huang's relationship with alleged Chinese spy

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Nvidia has opened an investigation into co-founder and CEO Jensen Huang's relationship with a businesswoman who intelligence agencies say is a Chinese spy.

U.S. intelligence agencies have expressed concerns that the woman, Alice Huang, who is affiliated with a cloud company and an Nvidia customer called Megaspeed, has been passing information to Beijing, according to reports in The New York Times and the Financial Times.

Huang told Nvidia staff in an email on Sunday that he had an affair with the woman and that Nvidia's board of directors had opened an independent investigation into the spying allegations.

"I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did," Huang wrote in the message. "I am deeply sorry."

An Nvidia spokesperson said that Huang was committed to continuing as CEO of Nvidia without interruption.

In a statement, the Nvidia board said it would investigate the allegations that Huang had been the target of a foreign intelligence effort.

"While the Board has no reason to believe that either national security or NVIDIA secrets were compromised, the Board's inquiry will examine all available information," Nvidia's board said in a statement.

Read Huang's email to Nvidia employees and the company board of directors' statement here.

The allegations come as Nvidia has become the center of a trade dispute between China and the U.S. as AI is seen as increasingly critical to national security.

The U.S. restricts exports of Nvidia's most advanced AI chips to China, though it has approved exports of certain older, slower models.

Megaspeed, which Ms. Huang is affiliated with, has been accused of helping restricted Chinese companies acquire Nvidia AI chips. Nvidia representatives previously said that Megaspeed had already been investigated for evading export controls.

Nvidia's Huang has pushed for broader AI chip exports to China, saying it is better for the U.S. if the world uses and builds on American technology. He has also aligned with President Donald Trump on American artificial intelligence policy and its infrastructure buildout.

In his note to staff, Huang said he was "proud and grateful" to be an American.

Three House Republican committee chairs sent a letter to Huang on Wednesday asking him to sit for a closed-door briefing with lawmakers on Nov. 18.

Nvidia reports third-quarter earnings on Nov. 17 and will hold a major conference in Washington, D.C., at the end of the month.

'There is plenty of work to do': New Sheffield Wednesday boss Dave Challinor suffers FA Cup exit at Oldham Athletic

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

The central defender, 24, popped up to head home Latics’ 110th-minute winner, much to the delight of home fans who see Sutton as ‘one of our own’.

Here are our player ratings – and the fact the man we picked out as Crawley’s best operator wasn’t even on the pitch in the first half says it all…

Vito Mannone: 3.5 – The experienced goalkeeper was at fault for Wimborne’s opener in the first half as his throw fell short of its target and the home side capitalised. Did produce a good save while Wimborne led 2-0.

Kellan Gordon: 3 – Drove down the right hand side well at times and got into good crossing positions but his deliveries failed to beat the first man on a number of occasions or were too close to the keeper.

Charlie Barker: 4 – The 23 year old was in midfield once again and battled well at times winning some of his aerial duels but was quite sloppy on the ball throughout the game.

Kai Jennings: 3 – The Wimbledon loanee had a tough time in midfield but did have a good effort in the first half while it was 1-0 which flashed wide.

Josh Flint: 6 – Gave the Reds some momentum when he came on at half-time, driving his team up the pitch with his marauding runs and was by far Crawley’s best player.

Nathan Tinsdale: 4 – The youngster struggled in difficult circumstances like many of his teammates.

Pemi Aderoju: 4 – Was more effective up front than Orsi, winning flick ons from the lofted balls up front. Had one great chance five minutes into the second half which he volleyed straight at the keeper.

Ben Heneghan: 3.5 – The 6ft 3 centre back came on to make his debut but failed to make any sort of impact.

Michael Jackson’s ‘Thriller’ Scares Up Highest Rank on Billboard 200 Albums Chart in More Than 40 Years

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

The classic, which spent 37 weeks at No. 1 in 1983-84, surged sparked by Halloween.

Michael Jackson’s Thriller vaults 13-2 on the Billboard 200 (dated Nov. 14), scoring its highest rank on the list since the spring of 1984, when it logged its 37th and final week at No. 1 on the April 14-dated list. Thriller continues to hold the record for the most weeks atop the list for an album by a singular artist. (Only the film soundtrack to West Side Story spent more weeks at No. 1: 54, in 1962-63.)

The Billboard 200 chart ranks the most popular albums of the week in the U.S. based on multi-metric consumption as measured in equivalent album units, compiled by Luminate. Units comprise album sales, track equivalent albums (TEA) and streaming equivalent albums (SEA). Each unit equals one album sale, or 10 individual tracks sold from an album, or 2,500 ad-supported or 1,000 paid/subscription on-demand official audio and video streams generated by songs from an album. The new Nov. 14, 2026-dated chart will be posted in full on Billboard‘s website on Nov. 10. For all chart news, follow @billboard and @billboardcharts on both X and Instagram.

In the tracking week of Oct. 30 through Nov. 5 — thus, including Halloween — Thriller earned 55,000 equivalent album units (up 89%), boosted by seasonal spooky streams of the set’s title track, as well as new physical versions of the album that were released. Of that unit total, SEA units comprise 32,000 (up 37%, equaling 34.34 million on-demand official streams of the set’s songs; it moves 14-5 on Top Streaming Albums), album sales comprise 22,000 (up 335%; it rises 14-3 on Top Album Sales) and TEA units comprise 1,000 (up 30%).

On Oct. 30, Thriller was reissued across three new vinyl variants (two color editions and a liquid-filled version) and a cassette tape. The album’s 22,000 sales for the week mark its largest single-week sales sum in nearly four years, since it shifted 23,000 (Dec. 31, 2022-dated list).

Notably, of Thriller’s 22,000 sold in the latest tracking week, vinyl purchases account for 19,000 — the album’s largest sales week on vinyl since Luminate began electronically tracking sales in 1991.

With Thriller’s jump to No. 2, Jackson has his highest rank on the Billboard 200 in more than a decade, since the posthumous album Xscape debuted and peaked at No. 2 on the May 31, 2014-dated chart.

Notably, Thriller has appeared on the Billboard 200 each year since 2009, following Jackson’s death that June. It has resided in the top 20 each week since early May, with 15 consecutive weeks in the top 10 through August, spurred by the record-setting biopic Michael.

Robinhood Chain was built for tokenized stocks – But memecoins took over

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Gen Zs are rewriting the rules of wealth creation, increasingly trading the traditional financial playbook for a high-stakes game of chance.

From cryptocurrencies and memecoins to sports betting and prediction markets, the generation is exploring unconventional ways in pursuit of financial gains that once seemed possible only through years of saving and investing.

In this complex financial landscape, the line separating investing from gambling is blurring by the day. In fact, Ashley Embers recently highlighted how 37% of Gen Z respondents engage in sports betting, while 20% place bets online.

More concerningly, over half of Gen Z adults aged 18–29 reportedly redirected money originally intended for investments into sports betting over the past year, with 14% doing so several times a month.

This shift is also being reflected in a few corners of the crypto market, which is even better than memecoins.

One interesting example of this shift is Robinhood Chain.

When Robinhood introduced its Ethereum [ETH] Layer-2 network, the company planned to build a three-in-one infrastructure layer, which includes tokenized stocks, real-world assets [RWAs], and decentralized finance [DeFi] applications.

Expanding on this, Johann Kerbrat, SVP and General Manager of Crypto and International at Robinhood, said,

We’re bringing the best of traditional finance and DeFi together, and in doing so, expanding financial ownership to every corner of the globe.

The main goal of the team was to bring traditional financial markets on-chain to enable users to access tokenized versions of familiar assets through blockchain infrastructure.

However, the network’s early adoption took a complete U-turn from the initial goal. Rather than being primarily driven by tokenized equities and traditional financial products, Robinhood Chain attracted considerable attention from memecoin traders.

Expressing his opinion on the matter, Robinhood CEO Vlad Tenev said,

If you hold a memecoin, you just get stock tokens airdropped to you.

This has raised an interesting question: Is Robinhood building the next generation of tokenized financial markets, or has it accidentally created another destination for speculative crypto trading?

Tokenized equities were expected to be one of the Robinhood’s defining use cases. However, the initial trading activity suggested that traders have found another reason to use the network.

Per Bitquery’s analysis of Robinhood Chain’s trading activity between the 16th and the 28th of July 2026, memecoins accounted for approximately 93.5% of trading across 63.5 million transactions. Tokenized equities, meanwhile, represented just 6.5%.

CoinGecko also highlighted this shift in trend. As per its July 2026 assessment, memecoins accounted for 79.2% of Robinhood Chain’s DEX volume, whereas RWAs made up just 9.69%.

These reveal a considerable gap between Robinhood’s intended use case and actual market demand.

Robinhood Chain has also enabled creators to pair their memecoins with tokenized versions of established companies.

This means that tokenized equities are not necessarily being used for traditional investment purposes. Instead, they have become the trading currency through which users speculate on unrelated digital assets.

Similarly, Bloomberg reported that daily trading volume in pairs combining Robinhood stock tokens with memecoins had reached approximately $443 million in early September.

Yet, despite the speculative nature of its activity, Robinhood Chain has recorded impressive growth since its launch. CoinGecko’s Q3 2026 Crypto Industry Report highlighted the network as the third-most-active blockchain within just three months of its public launch.

In Q3 2026, Robinhood Chain generated more than $88.9 billion in trading volume, averaging approximately 9.5 million transactions per day.

Daily trading volume on its native decentralized exchanges also increased from approximately $1.3 million on launch day to a peak of $3.5 billion on the 4th of September.

The company also added approximately $25 million worth of Bitcoin to its corporate balance sheet in October, describing the purchase as an asset-diversification strategy.

These developments indicate that Robinhood’s broader ambitions extend well beyond memecoins.

However, memecoin trading on the Robinhood Chain is not without its risks.

Bitquery’s investigation found that approximately 84% of the nearly 15,000 tokens launched daily never traded again after their initial transaction. The investigation also revealed that three out of every four open trading positions were underwater.

Meanwhile, Fortune subsequently reported that trading volume in these memecoin-stock pairs had fallen by approximately 96% from its early-September peak.

This highlights how quickly speculative enthusiasm disappears. Yet, despite these concerns, it would be premature to conclude that memecoin activity is sabotaging Robinhood’s broader ambitions.

In fact, the silver lining here might be that speculation could be serving as an unexpected entry point into tokenized finance.

Though the company may have succeeded in getting traders through the door, whether they stay for tokenized stocks and RWAs, rather than the next viral memecoin, remains the bigger question on the table.

Ishika Kumari is a Crypto Analyst at AMBCrypto, specializing in regulatory developments, market dynamics, and blockchain’s real-world impact. She breaks down complex protocols and legislation into practical, easy-to-understand insights.

Burnley must sanction emotional Newcastle switch to fix key problem area

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Burnley have endured a nightmare beginning to the 2026/27 Championship campaign following relegation from the Premier League, as the January transfer window looks increasingly important for the Clarets to get right in each passing week.

Burnley were relegated with a whimper from the Premier League last season, but heading back into the Championship, Clarets fans would have naturally been optimistic about their chances, having been automatically promoted the three previous times they found themselves in the division.

However, finding a replacement for Scott Parker proved to be difficult, and in truth, drawn out for way too long, as Belgian head coach Nicky Hayen was handed a poison chalice upon his arrival at Turf Moor.

Hayen won just one of his 12 league games in charge in Lancashire, as well as being knocked out of the League Cup at the hands of League One Bradford City, as incoming Swiss boss Fabio Celestini will be hoping to be given until January to make some much-needed changes to Burnley ranks.

Following his swift appointment, Celestini will have had time to work with his squad in training and identify key areas of improvement, which, in the Clarets’ case right now, is all over the pitch.

However, some positions are more problematic than others, as the options between the sticks right now at Turf Moor aren’t wholly convincing.

Max Weiss started the campaign between the sticks, but after a few high-profile errors, the German was dropped in favour of French goalkeeper Gregoire Coudert, who joined from Brest in the summer.

Since replacing Weiss between the sticks, Coudert has not covered himself in much glory either, shipping four away to Lincoln City and being at fault for Queen’s Park Rangers’ equaliser in their 1-1 draw at Loftus Road, as a highly recognisable name ought to be a priority this January to fix this evident issue.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top 25 most demanding fanbases in British football right now

If the tide doesn’t turn at Burnley sooner rather than later, the Clarets are at risk of following in the footsteps of the likes of Sunderland, Luton Town, and Leicester City to suffer double relegations from the Premier League to League One in consecutive years.

A solid pair of hands between the sticks can be a game-changer, as Celestini must look to bring out-of-favour Newcastle United shot-stopper Nick Pope back to the club in January.

Pope, 34, worked his way up the Football League with the likes of Bury Town, Harrow Borough, Welling United, Charlton Athletic, Cambridge United, Aldershot Town, York City, and Bury before earning a move to Burnley in the summer of 2016, initially acting as deputy to Tom Heaton under Sean Dyche.

155 appearances across all competitions later, Pope had announced himself as one of the finest shot-stoppers in the country, earning ten caps for England during Jordan Pickford’s dominant era, and eventually joining Newcastle United in the summer of 2022 for around £10 million following Burnley’s relegation.

At 34, Pope found himself battling with Southampton outcast Aaron Ramsdale for the number one spot at St James’ Park last season under Eddie Howe, and now is firmly behind Lukáš Horníček in the pecking order.

With Ewen Jaouen and Mark Gillespie also vying for minutes under Mathias Jaissle, Pope’s Newcastle career appears to be coming to a quiet end, as the former England international ought to push for a return to Turf Moor this winter.

Despite his age, few can deny his outstanding shot-stopping ability, and right now, you’d surely do well to find a Burnley supporter who doesn’t believe Pope would walk straight into their side right now in the Championship.

Burnley need a hero, and someone to increase the mood around the place, and given Pope’s situation at Newcastle, he could be the perfect fit.

The only obvious stumbling block is likely his wages, which may be too steep for the Clarets to match, though they would be remiss not to try, as Celestini has one obvious goal heading into January.

Do you think Burnley should look to re-sign Nick Pope from Newcastle in January, or should they be looking elsewhere? Share your thoughts below!

Burnley are in desperate need of an uptick in form sooner rather than later; otherwise, they risk being cut adrift from the rest of the pack.

The January window will provide Celestini with the chance to make some much-needed changes to his squad, and it would be a surprise if a new goalkeeper wasn’t high on his list.

The upcoming window could be Burnley’s last roll of the dice to secure survival, as the Clarets cannot afford to let it pass them by.
