“We should have reacted better” | Frank admission won’t bring the points back | West Ham News

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Down in ninth place, outside even the play-off places, is hardly where West Ham, the pre-season favourites and the ‘big hitters descended into the Championship’ expected to find themselves after fifteen games. This, the most competitive of leagues, throws up unexpected results each and every week and West Ham have, simply, not been up to speed.

The week after week excuses of Nuno Espírito Santo have been replaced by the emotional, heart-on-his-sleeve reassurances of an albeit shell- shocked Scott Parker, but it is clear there’s a huge challenge ahead.

Scott Parker may have a more engaging style than Nuno but excuses only count for so long

Talk is cheap, there are only so many times we as fans we can be told that the team should have tried harder, prepared better, worked more, et cetera, et cetera, et cetera..before our eyes glaze over and disappointment gives way to anger – as was displayed at full time yesterday.

Defender Dinos Mavropanos, to his credit, takes his fair share of the blame himself for the defensive lapse that led to Blackburn’s second goal when he and his team mates switched off, anticipating a stoppage for Max Kilman’s head injury instead of playing to the whistle:

“I mean, yes, it’s tough, this league with no VAR coming around, it’s difficult to speak about these situations,” he explained

“I’m not going to blame the referee because at the end it’s a decision I can’t change. But again, I think we should have reacted better and not concede this kind of goal.” (whufc.com)

Exactly so, Dinos. However frank acceptances like this don’t bring the points back. Forget automatic promotion. Eleven points dropped in the last four games is relegation form, whichever way you look at it.

West Ham pay for the lack of competition for centre back places, as we’ve already pointed out repeatedly. Taking the blame for mistakes is one thing. Not repeating them is a whole other set of problems.

Two Neighboring $9.9 Million Oceanfront Homes Just Listed in Kauai’s South Shore—And They Can Form an Ultra-Luxe Compound

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Tucked into a private-club community in Hawaii called Kukui‘ula, two luxury homes just hit the market on Kauai's South Shore.

Set right on the ocean, and set among lush tropical vegetation, this walkable community is only 25-minute drive from Lihue Airport.

"The close proximity of everything is very convenient. You’re not having to spend a lot of time in the car,” says Pancho Sullivan of Hawaii Life, who is co-listing the two homes with Haunani Sullivan as well as Suzanne Harding and Jeff Skinner of Kukui‘ula Realty Group. The asking price sits at $9,900,000 each.

Both were built in 2025 and have never been occupied. Aepo Nei 18 and Aepo Nei 19 are neighboring 3,372-square-foot homes with four bedrooms and 4.5 baths each. They can be purchased together, to create a private compound, or by separate buyers.

Even better, the homes are fully furnished and stocked with items like bed and bath linens, beach towels, and everything you need to cook and serve in the kitchen, such as bowls, plates and glasses.

But it’s the design by Brazil-based Architects+CO that’s truly unique. Kauai Bahia Construction and their in-house material company, Kayu, handled all of the finish material production and millwork installation.

"Brazil is known for incredible resort-club communities," says Pancho. "We actually traveled to Brazil to look at some of the clubs they designed. It’s the same tropical feel (as Hawaii) that blends in really well with nature."

Aepo Nei 18’s layout features connected pavilions while Aepo Nei 19’s living spaces open onto a lawn courtyard. Design features at both include deep roof overhangs, walls of glass and multiple outdoor living spaces such as an infinity-edge pool; jacuzzi; swim-in, swim-out sauna; and in-water fire pit.

"They both have beautiful 180-degree ocean views looking across the Tom Weiskopf-designed golf course. The swim-in, swim-out dry sauna facing the ocean is a unique feature," says Pancho, adding that the usage of natural materials like lava stone, coral and teak "fit into the landscape very beautifully."

Referred to as the Garden Island, Kauai is home to about 73,000 people.

"We get the majority of the island’s sunshine down here," says Haunani, about the South Shore. "People flock here for the beaches and the sunshine, Hawaiian monk seals on the sand, and a lot of ocean activity including surfing.

"A lot of people that venture to Kauai are adventure seekers. We have great hiking. You can get on a boat and dive on the Nā Pali Coast and do ziplining and ATV tours."

Stand-up paddleboarding, diving and fishing are two other activities easy to do in this community.

Homeowners become club members with access to dining, a pool, spa (boasting a steam rook and lava-rock dry sauna), pickleball and tennis courts, and an 18-hole golf course.

"There’s an incredible gym with classes daily, from yoga to sound bathing to fitness training," says Pancho. "All of that is part of your club membership. It’s a great place to be in tune with nature and get healthy."

A 10-acre organic garden features a farm stand as well as opportunities to create floral bouquets. There’s also a conscious effort to honor Hawaiian history through cultural-heritage walks; sunrise ceremonies; hula performances; and classes in Hawaiian featherwork, quilting and basket weaving.

“Members who are part of the club become life-long friends,” says Haunani. “They’re going to each other’s second homes and weddings. They travel around the world together.”

The community also organizes annual golf and pickleball tournaments for members, and three golf courses are within a seven-minute drive.

Another incentive is that the community’s zoned as a Vacation Destination Area.

This means properties can join the short-term rental pool, for minimum seven-night stays.

"Some of the owners put their homes in the rental pool and block out time for themselves and their families. That’s a great added benefit," says Haunani, as few club communities on Kauai allow this.

The Sullivans think the buyer will likely hail from the West Coast, enticed by a direct flight from hubs in cities like Los Angeles, San Francisco, Seattle, Phoenix and Denver that arrive on the island by mid-day.

“Kukui‘ula is a great place for families to gather and spend time together. We see a lot of members bring their entire families so they can vacation together multiple times per year,” says Pancho.

“With all these activities it’s easy to curate an incredible vacation with the convenience of being on property. A lot of people enjoy coming here and decompressing.”

Based in Milwaukee, Kristine Hansen writes for TravelandLeisure.com, ArchitecturalDigest.com, Vogue.com, and other outlets. She’s also the author of “Frank Lloyd Wright’s Wisconsin: How America’s Most Famous Architect Found Inspiration in His Home State.” She lives in a 1924 bungalow.

This weekend, the Harper’s Bazaar cover star headed to Las Vegas to catch Metallica’s Life Burns Faster show at the Sphere, spending an evening soaking up some heavy metal with her sons, Eddy and Nelson Angélil.

Spotted in a box, Dion was captured rocking out to the band’s “Nothing Else Matters,” singing along and playing air guitar (even when she was the only one standing up in her section). Rather than wearing a classic band T-shirt, she dressed up for the occasion in a blue suit, featuring a fitted blazer with a wrap detail at the waist. Dion, who hadn’t performed a full-scale concert since her 2022 stiff-person syndrome diagnosis, hosted a total of 16 shows throughout the month, with a starry crowd that rivaled Metallica’s (think Rihanna and Michelle Obama).

She honored her host city with a wardrobe full of pieces from the country’s top couture houses, including a dramatic black Dior gown made custom by Jonathan Anderson and a dusty-pink Givenchy cape. In May, she will head back to Paris for an additional 10 nights.

macOS 27.3 could change how you grant AI agents Full Disk Access, here’s how

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Last month, Apple announced it’d be tightening privacy controls in a future macOS update, in response to AI agents requesting Full Disk Access then going rogue with users data without them being fully aware. Now, we have an idea of how the changes might look.

In its blog post, Apple announced that apps will require “very explicit user action” to have Full Disk Access on your Mac. At the time of the announcement, Apple didn’t make it super clear what that would actually mean. Now, we have an idea of what’s to come.

Code sifter pdfu posted on X that Apple will be getting rid of a clever loophole that developers could use to make it easier for users to enable Full Disk Access. In current versions of macOS, if an app requests a file that would require Full Disk Access, the system denies the request and logs the app as an option in the Settings app for enabling Full Disk Access.

At that point, developers often prompt users to open that section of the Settings app and flip the toggle.

Starting as soon as macOS 27.3, apps that request files outside of the permission scope will no longer get logged, and as a result, won’t show up as an option for quickly toggling Full Disk Access, per leaker pdfu.

macOS 27.3 starts implementing changes to Full Disk Access (FDA). Here’s what ‘very explicit user action’ may mean.When an app tries to read a protected file without FDA, macOS doesn’t prompt the user to grant permission. Instead, it automatically denies access and records that… https://t.co/zjUuEh7EIb

At the moment, developers can still request users to drag an app icon to the Settings panel to enable Full Disk Access, even once the feature flag for the aforementioned change is enabled. To me, it still doesn’t quite feel like you’ll need “very explicit user action” to enable Full Disk Access, but it’s possible this will change in the future. This is all still in development.

All in all, I’m not fully convinced that this solves the core problem. Developers are requesting Full Disk Access because they’d like to tap into things like iMessage, and there hasn’t been a better way of doing so. As long as that demand is there, users will likely still have to enable Full Disk Access and risk all sorts of other data, just so they can share their texts with an agent.

Granted, pdfu also mentions the idea of Private Folders, which could potentially allow users to grant scoped access to just the iMessage database folder. It’s unclear if that’ll be possible since there’s little detail on how Private Folders will work. If that’s the case, then hopefully Full Disk Access can stop being the default permission you have to provide for any personal data.

Michael Carrick has painfully awkward exchange with interviewer over Man United future

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Carrick appeared to be unimpressed by questioning from Sky Sports after United’s remarkable 4-4 draw with Aston Villa as he gave a series of terse responses

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Michael Carrick endured an awkward end to a post-match interview as he had an excruciating exchange about his future as Manchester United manager.

Carrick had just watched his United side fight back from 4-2 down in injury time to snatch the unlikeliest of 4-4 draws with Aston Villa, having earlier come back from being 2-0 behind inside five minutes.

It was a real mixed display from the Red Devils, who showed plenty of heart and character but had put themselves in bad positions due to consistently disastrous defending.

The result – combined with their incredible comeback victory against Roma in midweek – has probably saved Carrick’s job, although his United side still head into this international break in the Premier League’s bottom three, lying 18th in the table.

His long-term future appears to very much still be up in the air and his post-match interview on Sky Sports with reporter Patrick Davison was an awkward watch.

Carrick firstly bristled slightly when asked if United are looking at a relegation fight, saying: “No, we’re in November. I don’t like this situation we’re in, I don’t like the results we’ve had but we can definitely do something about it.”

From there, things got worse, as Davison asked a lengthy question suggesting there were clearly going to be conversations about his future during the international break over the next couple of weeks, to which Carrick simply responded: "Is there?"

When it was suggested that one would assume so, Carrick tersely said: “Would you?” and when he was pushed for what he thought about his situation, the Man United boss said to Davison: “You’re telling me, what do you think”.

After that, the interviewer specified that he wanted to know what Carrick expected to happen in the next couple of weeks and he then gave a longer answer admitting that it wasn’t a good situation, the team haven’t won enough games but that he expects to move forward and keep working on the things they’ve been working on until this point.

Back in the Sky Sports studio, pundit Roy Keane was asked for his thoughts on the interview as a whole and the former Man United midfielder compared the current boss to a politician.

He said: “It’s obviously difficult for the manager, he’s under pressure no doubt. He talks a lot but doesn’t say much – maybe that’s a tactic. He’s like a politician.

“He’s clearly under pressure and says he knows there are things to work on but he better get moving on that... This is their worst start in however many years.”

Keane was also critical of United’s “dreadful” defending, although he praised the heart they showed to twice come back from two goals behind.

He added: “I think we’ve said previously there have been question marks over this team but they’re showing a bit of fight, a bit of desire and, as bad as the game was from a footballing point of view, they kept going to the end.

“So, on the one hand, you say dreadful to give up four goals at home, but they kept going, so we have to give them a little bit of praise.

“In terms of the bigger picture, it’s that rollercoaster of watching United. Some bits were so, so bad. Defensively the way it started, the start of the second half, but they found a way to get a point in a game where they looked dead and buried.

“Still nowhere near good enough to try and get towards the middle of the table.”

Battlefield 6 Loses Court Case Over Ban

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Michael Manning (He/Him) has been enamored with video games since he first played the Sonic Classic Collection as a child. With decades of experience in gaming and a full tour as a Gaming Journalist, Michael is ready to step up to the plate. From AAA to the smallest passion projects, his motto is that every piece of ar

Modern Warfare 4's launch party is still going, with a free play period for DMZ ready to rake in new players.

Back in early August, a Battlefield 6 player named Rychadv complained on Reddit that his profile has been randomly, unexplainably banned after a period of inactivity, with no support levied from EA after attempts at appeal. Today, he revealed that he took EA to court over the case and won, regaining access to his account effective immediately.

While most don’t carefully read the terms and conditions many online games ask of you, the agreement does serve as somewhat legally binding, acting as a way for the studio running the game to establish when and if they can pull your plug. Cheating, improper communications use, or any step out of line with the terms laid out can be used as a justification to temporarily, or permanently, ban you.

The odd part that swung this case fully in Rychadv’s favor was the lack of an actual motive. For no discernible reason, after an extended break from the game, DICE had simply banned his account, with no means of reacquiring it or fighting the ban. Especially on a full-priced title like Battlefield 6, this type of hiccup is strictly unacceptable, potentially costing Rychadv a whole new copy if he didn’t drag DICE to court.

Bans need to be carefully considered by studios before they’re implemented; otherwise, they risk both stepping on toes and unjustly kicking out solid chunks of their populace. The threat of bans, more so than the bans themselves, is often enough to keep the problem children in line. We’ve all had a mouthy, rude player on the team, the more invested in the game they are, the less likely they are to get heinous.

Without them, and without proper explanations for bans, companies can get into more than just PR trouble.

This widely depends on both the player culture and ban frequency of the game itself, and is far more common in free-to-play titles where accounts can quickly rise and fall. For full-priced experiences, developers need to be a bit more careful with the ban hammer, and they especially need to make sure any perceived bannable offenses actually happened.

The gradual move away from physical media for the majority of the gaming industry makes the threat of random bans a bit more serious, especially with cases like Rychadv’s still out in the open. It’s not just multiplayer titles that are always online, and who's to say some studio couldn’t kick you out for what you do in a single-player game in the future, even when it's only affecting your copy?

This is definitely stepping into the speculative, but it's important to remember that digital titles aren’t really yours; you simply buy a license from the studio to play. Without the promise of physical media, there’s a certain formlessness to the games we buy, like they could slip away at any moment.

With no promise of actual ownership, situations like Rychadv’s could happen more and more, putting the onus of action onto the players to hold studios over the fire a bit. When you agree to the terms of service, make sure to read up on it. While you’re playing by the studio’s rules, they need to respect them as well, especially for titles with a price tag.

Improper use of bans could lead to more legal disputes like Rychadv’s, as players become more willing to hold studios to their word.

Deadlock is starting to suffer from the same fate as other free-to-play games, with bot farmers crawling from the woodwork.

Carrick’s Man Utd have gone from the Prem’s most underwhelming team to the most bizarre

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

For the second time this week, Manchester United looked to be down and out but produced a stunning comeback that might just keep Michael Carrick in a job. Maybe?

On Tuesday they went 2-0 down to Roma in the Champions League before dramatically winning 3-2. But the respite looked like it could be short lived when they quickly went two goals down to Aston Villa on Sunday.

Goals by Alysson and Nicolas Jackson inside the first four minutes meant it was the earliest United had ever been 2-0 down in the Premier League. One for the record books and not the kind that Carrick needed.

Nevertheless, United clawed their way back into the game to make it 2-2 with goals from Bruno Fernandes and Youri Tielemans before the break. Was another come-from-behind win on its way?

No. United conceded another couple of goals within the first 10 minutes of the second half to be facing a two-goal deficit again.

And yet, somehow, they managed to emerge from the game with a point, coming back to secure a 4-4 draw.

Joshua Zirkzee and Patrick Dorgu both scored in stoppage time to bring United back from the brink in even more dramatic fashion.

It was a bonkers game which has unsurprisingly churned out a few eye-catching facts and figures.

Most notably among them, this is the first time ever in Premier League history that a team has been behind by two goals and levelled the game twice.

Should Carrick keep his job, he may well look back at this week, with the spirit United have shown, as a turning point.

If not, he’ll have gone out as the maker of a piece of Premier League history.

The fact of the matter remains United have only won one of their first 10 games in the league this season. They go into the international break in the relegation zone. It was impressive, but it might not be enough.

Whatever happens from here, United’s latest comeback served as the latest reminder to expect the unexpected with this team.

For the first few games this season, they seriously underwhelmed. No doubt about it, they still are underwhelming, but they’re starting to do it in (almost?) entertaining fashion.

Last-gasp drama, unexpected comebacks, and is that a glimmer of fighting spirit there?

This season hasn’t been United’s first crisis in recent times. But it might just becoming a different kind of crisis. Who said we were running out of scripts?

What United do from here is anyone’s guess – on the pitch and off it.

They’re certainly keeping us on our toes.

Mass Effect Director Thinks The Series Will Make A “Triumphant Return”

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

BioWare veteran and Mass Effect trilogy director Casey Hudson is “optimistic” about the future of the series thanks to all of the support from fans.

This year’s N7 Day was, at one point, supposed to be a time for celebrating Mass Effect and looking toward the future for its grand return. Thanks to EA’s gutting decision to shut down BioWare and cancel what we now know to be Mass Effect: Universe, N7 Day ended up feeling a lot more like a funeral.

As depressing as the state of Mass Effect is, though, fans have at least made their love and support for the series incredibly clear through petitions, events, and even hand-made graveyards outside EA’s office. That dedication really came through yesterday, so much so that it made the original trilogy’s director feel hopeful about Mass Effect’s potential return.

Over on Twitter, former BioWare developer Casey Hudson shared a post to celebrate N7 Day, noting that it “doesn’t feel right” to say ‘happy’ this time around. As grim as the vibes are this year, Hudson offered a bit of hope for fans, as he said that he’s “optimistic that Mass Effect will live on”.

#SaveMassEffect is going viral, with support from the Prime Video showrunner as the fanbase calls for EA to bring back the franchise.

The director continued, pointing out that when (not if) the series makes its “triumphant return”, it will be because of all the love that players around the world have shared. Hudson seemingly thinks that the support that fans have shown for the series will be enough to push EA into actually doing something with it in the future.

Hopefully the Mass Effect TV show will follow in Fallout’s footsteps and be good enough to draw in an even bigger audience that simply can’t be ignored.

Hudson’s message is especially notable considering he’s the director of Star Wars: Fate of the Old Republic, which is in itself bringing back a franchise that BioWare previously worked on. It just goes to show that, while Universe and the team responsible for the series are sadly gone, there could still be a bright future ahead for Mass Effect.

Cut It Out! Hollywood’s Red Carpets Are Finding New Ways to Show Skin

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Kim Kardashian has never met a cutout she didn’t like. The reality star and fashion provocateur has made something of a signature out of showing skin in unexpected places—she loves an exposed hip, a plunging neckline, and a dress that appears to defy the laws of physics. At last night’s 2026 LACMA Art+Film Gala in Los Angeles, she was in familiar territory, wearing a burgundy sequined Gucci gown by Demna that was all business from the front, with its high neck, long sleeves, and sculpted silhouette, and decidedly less so from the side. A dramatic slash across the waist revealed an expanse of skin above the hip.

And she was far from alone. If the evening’s red carpet was anything to go by, Hollywood’s appetite for the naked dress has evolved into something more architectural and off-beat. Forget the romantically diaphanous or the all-out sheer, the latest approach to showing skin is all about strategic exposure. Think a dramatically placed opening, a vertiginous slit, and plunging, crack-skimming backs. Sometimes, what a dress conceals is just as important as what it reveals.

Leading the charge, of course, was the cohort of celebrities dressed by Gucci, where Demna has established a provocative, high-octane strain of glamour. Rosie Huntington-Whiteley wore a black, long-sleeved custom gown with exaggerated shoulders and a severe, almost monastic front that gave way to a completely exposed back and butt, with a daring slit extending to the hip, fastened with a small jeweled detail. Restrained from one angle, outrageously risqué from another.

Fellow model Amber Valletta also opted for custom Gucci, in a black column gown with a plunging open back, a silver ornamental detail, and a thigh-baring slit that extended almost to her waist. Emily Ratajkowski chose a white draped Gucci number with a plunging neckline and dramatically open sides, paired with a sky-high leg slit.

There were plenty more variations on the theme. Alix Earle embraced the evening’s maximalist mood in a silver Gucci, disco-ready halter gown dripping with strands of shimmering embellishment, with a thigh-high slit emerging from beneath the metallic fringe. Olympic skier Lindsey Vonn also turned to Gucci, wearing a glittering, one-shouldered purple gown with an asymmetrical neckline and a dramatic slit that revealed a sheer black-stockinged leg. Candice Swanepoel took a similarly sparkling approach in a silver, crystal-strewn gown with a plunging neckline and two daringly high slits, with more sparkle cascading over her legs.

Not everyone was in Gucci, though. Madelaine Petsch opted for a look from Georges Hobeika’s spring 2027 collection, with a black halter-neck bodice that offered a hint of side-boob, and which plunged almost to the waist before meeting a cream floral-embroidered pencil skirt.

The naked dress has long been a Hollywood fixture. Body-conscious designs are beloved by the Kardashian-Jenner clan, having a deep understanding of the impact of a well-placed flash of flesh. But then there’s people like Anya Taylor-Joy, a champion of the sensuous and barely there, keeping a more ghostly and romantic bent with winter-white corsetry by Dilara Findikoglu and forays into vampy Tom Ford. This new iteration, however, uses precision cuts and dramatic contrasts to draw the eye to particular parts of the body.

With Demna’s Gucci helping to usher in a new mood of all-out, unabashed glamour, it seems the red carpet is entering its next undressed era.

Melty New Puzzle Game From Shotgun King Creators Burns Tetris At Both Ends

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Game JamIndie GamesLudum DarePuzzleTetris

You live through life fending off all kinds of intrusive thoughts. Resisting the urge to kick every tire icicle. Plucking at grass, which wasnât bothering no one. Lighting a really tall candle and giving it a nudge just to make sure it couldnât possibly fall over otherwise. A new game from the creators of Shotgun King will free you of those inhibitions. A reimagining of Tetris that demands that you ignite the wick right through to the stem. As much as Hot Wax latches itself to an impulse, it still demands you carefully consider your next move, lest you burn yourself out later.

Presented deep within some unilluminated, dungeon keep, Hot Wax has you kicking off a summoning ritual with the use of magical candles. While tetromino shaped, these blocks do not extinguish themselves upon forming a horizontal line. Instead they must be burned from the top down. Every couple of pieces, the player is given a small ball of fire, which will carry along the path of the candle. Lighting any other exposed twine it brushes up against. However, remaining blocks of wax will tumble down when separated, a la Mr. Driller, and the ember will be snuffed out if smothered. Leaving you with a big basin of impenetrable goo.

Hot Wax requires you to rewire your Tetris instincts. The default practice of neatly arranging your blocks is efficiency for efficiencyâs sake. What you really want to do is create a safe path for the ember to do as much arson as possible. The rules differ slightly between modes as well. In the standard ritual, the flame only progresses one block per turn, though there is an opportunity to nab devilish power ups. In other modes, the fire will scorch right through, not just an immediate gratification but a helpful visual aid for seeing the game logic in real time.

Created by Shotgun King developers PUNKCAKE Delicieux, Hot Wax was born out of a 2024 Ludum Dare game jam. Like Kingâs blasting of chess, Wax comes at Tetris from underneath. Taking the malleable shape of it and working it with its fingers. Itâs reminiscent of the early dreamscape days of the genre, where Pipe Dreams tried to put a blue collar spin on the hit, while Alexey Pajitnov himself wondered “What if I gave Tetris a fancy little hat.” Itâs a comfortable space for PUNKCAKE, who will be hitting word jumbles the same way with If Letters Had Pants, Would They Wear Them Like This?

Roy Keane criticises Manchester United star after thrilling Aston Villa draw

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Roy Keane said the form of goalkeeper Senne Lammens is ‘hurting’ Manchester United after a thrilling 4-4 draw with Aston Villa.

Lammens endured another difficult game as Michael Carrick’s side conceded four goals at home to Villa.

The Belgian shot-stopper was nutmegged by Alysson inside two minutes and then could only parry a shot into the path of Nicolas Jackson who doubled Villa’s lead on four minutes.

The writing appeared to be on the wall for under-fire manager Carrick but United responded and were level at half-time thanks to Bruno Fernandes’ penalty and Youri Tielemans’ strike against his former club.

Sign up to our daily football newsletter, pick your team and get the latest news sent directly to your inbox every morning.

Aston Villa once again opened up a two-goal lead early into the second half, John McGinn scoring a penalty before Leon Goretzka poked home the visitors’ fourth.

But Manchester United scored two injury-time goals through substitutes Joshua Zirkzee and Patrick Dorgu to avoid defeat and salvage an unlikely point.

Despite two spirited fightbacks, an eighth league game without a win leaves the Red Devils in the relegation zone heading into the November international break.

Former Manchester United captain Keane was left with wildly mixed emotions after the game, criticising the poor defensive display but praising the team for showing ‘a bit of fight and desire’.

‘There have been question marks over this team showing a bit of fight, a bit of desire,’ Keane told Sky Sports. ‘And as bad as the game was from a football point of view they kept going until the end.

Use AI to go deeper into the stories you care about – powered by Metro and trusted publications.

‘They kept going, so you have to give them a little bit of praise. But in terms of the bigger picture it’s that rollercoaster of watching United.

‘Some bits were so, so bad. Defensively, the start to the first half, the start to the second half, but they found a way to get a point when they looked dead and buried.

‘Ultimately nowhere near good enough to try to get towards the middle of the table. They can do a lot better than what they’re doing. We saw evidence of that last year, when they went on the run they went in.

‘They’ve lost Casemiro, an important, experienced player for them. They certainly should be doing a lot better.

"You've got to be hard to beat" ❌Roy Keane talks about Manchester United lacking the "basics" pic.twitter.com/TnkdbchtcG

‘We go back to some of the basics. The way they’re starting games, the way they’re coming out for the second half. There are things they can improve on.

‘They’re probably lacking a bit of confidence but if you’re giving up four goals at home, you make it so hard for yourself.

‘When they were on a good run of results last year, the goalkeeper was doing very well, making a lot of big saves, he’s not doing that. Bruno was on fire, he’s not at his very best at the moment.

‘The goalkeeper has to do better. It doesn’t help if your goalkeeper isn’t making saves, that hurts the team a lot.

‘So there are some basic footballing reasons… but to be in the bottom three, playing for Man Utd… this should be hurting them.’

Following the November international break, Manchester United return to action with a daunting trip to Anfield to face arch-rivals Liverpool.

Aston Villa, meanwhile, who stay 10th after twice blowing a two-goal lead at Old Trafford, face relegation battlers Sunderland next.

Trans Thai Women Stole Tourist’s $600,000 Watch Over $60 Dispute—They ‘Thought It Was Fake’

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

A Chinese tourist says two women took his diamond-covered Patek Philippe and passport from his Pattaya hotel room. He valued the watch at roughly $600,000. When they demanded about $600 to return his belongings, he paid but only got his passport back. Police recovered the watch and arrested two suspects early Sunday morning. The suspects told police they were owed 2,000 baht (~ $60) and thought the watch was fake. The tourist reported the theft November 7 from room 309 at the Z8 Hotel. He said two transgender women took the watch and passport, then demanded 20,000 baht to return them. After collecting the cash, they returned the passport and left with the watch. The silver-colored, diamond-set Patek Philippe PP5719G can actually list at $725,000. The two suspects were arrested early in the morning of November 8. They claimed they’d just taken the watch figuring it was worth what they were owed (what they were owed for you can leave to your imagination). I’ve written about a guest who arrived at a Marriott wearing a $24,000 Rolex but hadn’t booked the night of his arrival. He fell asleep in the lobby waiting for a room, and someone took it right off his wrist. And in the stranger hotel watch recovery, two Rolexes taken from guests at Encore Las Vegas were concealed inside the women who stole them. A reader’s response to that story evokes Pulp Fiction: I don’t think I would ever wear the watch again. There is not enough disinfectant in the world. Now I can’t get images of the thieves looking just like Christopher Walken out of my mind. Y’all be careful on your travels out there! Police recovered the watch and arrested two suspects early Sunday morning. The suspects told police they were owed 2,000 baht (~ $60) and thought the watch was fake.

The tourist reported the theft November 7 from room 309 at the Z8 Hotel. He said two transgender women took the watch and passport, then demanded 20,000 baht to return them. After collecting the cash, they returned the passport and left with the watch.

The silver-colored, diamond-set Patek Philippe PP5719G can actually list at $725,000.

Man United are a rotten, shambolic mess. They lack intelligence, look uncoached and fumbling, failing Michael Carrick is not the man to arrest this trauma, writes IAN LADYMAN

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Published: 14:32 EDT, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 20:25 EDT, 8 November 2026

Manchester United do not have a football team, more a collection of adrenaline junkies. Over time, it will not be enough.

So deficient are Michael Carrick's players that they surely can't keep their manager in a job for much longer. For the most part United are shambolic, a mess. At times they play football so devoid of intelligence and competence that it's almost impossible to fathom. They look uncoached.

Yet here they were for the second night running at Old Trafford, completing a lap of honour at full-time while their supporters sang the name of Carrick as he completed his own journey in front of the Stretford End towards the tunnel.

United – it's worth pointing out – are third bottom of the Premier League. They haven't won in the league since August and have conceded more goals across all competitions – 30 – than any team bar Crystal Palace in Europe's top divisions.

But at Old Trafford they will cling to anything to help them look in the mirror without laughing and currently the currency is comebacks. There was one against Roma in the Champions League last Tuesday and here – quite remarkably – there were two in one game.

None of it should really mean anything in the grand scheme. United are rotten, capable of playing effectively only when something gives them an energy spike with which to roll. It makes for quite hypnotic theatre but has inadequacy and desperation at its core.

Patrick Dorgu (centre) scored a late equaliser as troubled Man United drew 4-4 vs Aston Villa

Dorgu scored in the 96th-minute with this header but United sit 18th in the Premier League

Despite the draw, Michael Carrick's position as United head coach looks increasingly doubtful

It won't sustain them or indeed their fumbling, failing manager. But for the moment it's what they have and what they are.

MANCHESTER UNITED (4-2-3-1): Lammens 4; Mazraoui 4, Yoro 6, Maguire 5 (Heaven 46, 5), Dalot 3 (Dorgu 66, 8); Tielemans 6 (Mount 66, 5), Mainoo 6; Amad 5 (Lacey 66, 7), Fernandes 6, Cunha 7; Mbeumo 5 (Zirkzee 83, 7)

Goals: Fernandes (16 pen), Tielemans (44), Zirkzee (90+2), Dorgu (90+6)

ASTON VILLA (4-2-3-1): Suzuki 6; Harwood-Bellis 5, Lindelof 6, Torres 6, Maatsen 4; Goretzka 6 (Mbaye 78, 6), Kamara 4; Alysson 7 (Barkley 71, 6), McGinn 8 (Hemmings 71, 6), Manzambi 6; Jackson 6 (Madjo 86)

Goals: Alysson (1), Jackson (4), McGinn (48 pen), Goretzka (53)

Here, they twice trailed by two, at the start of both halves. They were 2-0 down after only four minutes and 4-2 behind after 53. Somehow they managed not to lose.

The two goals United scored in added time at the end of the game came from absolutely nowhere. On the touchline Carrick – free from total humiliation for the second time in six days – raised a single finger to the sky.

In the directors' box – where the TV companies seem to have a camera permanently trained these days – fist pumps were in evidence from those in United blazers.

This was another uncomfortable afternoon for Messrs Ratcliffe, Wilcox and Berrada. A collection of new banners in the crowd made feelings known.

For now, Carrick is being spared any of the blame. That is strange and wrong but tells us much about the confused nature of the modern United. If they blame one of their own then they really do have nowhere left to go in search of self-respect.

United's opening against Roma last week had been traumatic enough. This was even worse but the root of it was desperately familiar – incompetent football.

The hapless United full-back Diogo Dalot was to blame for the first goal as Villa's Brazilian Alysson blasted his shot through the Portuguese's limp challenge. Later – after conceding a second half penalty – Dalot was booed off by his own fans.

That was painful and unedifying but completely understandable. Dalot has played more than 250 games for United yet has never even been good enough.

Aston Villa took the lead after just 59 seconds when Alysson (centre) scored at Old Trafford

Nicolas Jackson (centre) doubled the visitors' lead on four minutes as they made a dream start

Bruno Fernandes pulled one back for the hosts with a penalty on 16 minutes in a crazy start

For goal two, goalkeeper Senne Lammens was culpable as he pushed Alysson's shot into the path of Nicolas Jackson.

Once again United were in sink or swim territory. Once again, amazingly, they kept their heads above water – at least for a while.

Boubacar Kamara's 13th minute challenge on Matheus Cunha was stupid as the Brazilian was heading away from goal. But the Bruno Fernandes penalty that followed was calm and precise and the shot of belief United needed was theirs.

For the next 20 minutes or so, Carrick's team played well off the back of it. It was the only part of their game that will look good on the highlights. The United front four pressed and harried the Villa defence to the extent that Unai Emery's team couldn't get up the field.

Villa keeper Zion Suzuki saved well from a Cunha curler while subsequent shots from Fernandes, Kobbie Mainoo and Amad Diallo took deflections and narrowly missed the target.

At some stage it felt as though United's energy must drop. Over time it did. But then they equalised.

Moments after seeing Suzuki save one of his own efforts, Amad found space to pull the ball back for Youri Tielemans who placed his first United goal in from 18 yards against his old club. Carrick's team deserved to be level at that stage and had played some enterprising football to get there. But their self-harm gene surfaced again before long.

Youri Tielemans (right) brought United level before half-time with this strike vs his old club

John McGinn put Villa ahead again just minutes after half-time from the penalty spot

Leon Goretkza (left) made it 4-2 to Villa with this close-range strike as they looked set to win

Joshua Zirkzee (left) pulled one back in second-half stoppage-time with this finish for United

Alysson had almost scored with the last big moment of the first half and in the 47th minute he drew an utterly dumb foul from Dalot and John McGinn scored the penalty firmly.

United are beyond compare in the Premier League when it comes to this kind of thing. Who else in the top flight concedes the kind of goals they do? A case in point came in the 53rd minute.

McGinn's free-kick was heading across goal and away when United defender Leny Yoro inexplicably headed it towards his own goal. Lammens somehow touched the ball on to the bar but when it dropped Leon Goretzka poked it in.

That was the 16th goal United had conceded in the last five games and this time the attempt at a fightback was more muted.

There were some half chances – shots from Cunha and Fernandes – and some territory in which to play.

But this was a game Villa were now able to control rather better and they looked set to ease home until Patrick Dorgu crossed for Joshua Zirkzee to ease a goal in from out of nothing in the 2nd minute of added time.

Old Trafford was emptying fast by the time Dorgu headed in a 96th equaliser from a corner. But by then the collective blood was up once more and it was enough.

Villa appealed for a foul on goalkeeper Suzuki and it's true Lammens had gone looking for him. Was the contact between the two 'keepers significant enough to warrant VAR intervention? Probably not. United had their draw and they had a point.

If the season ended today, they would be down. Yet they were cheered off. Some things you just can't make up.

The catch in recovering a hacked crypto wallet: the thief may still have a key

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Keplr’s documented backups can reduce login dependence, but they cannot establish that a potentially exposed key is safe.

A crypto wallet that lets users sign in with Google or Apple can still lock them out when its login provider stops working. Web3Auth’s security pause has exposed that dependency, alongside a more serious problem: some Keplr users’ private keys may have been compromised. In the wallet app Keplr, a previously saved, usable private key offers a documented route independent of Google login. It does not establish that an incident-affected key remains safe.

On Nov. 7, @keplrwallet reported unauthorized transactions on some accounts created with Google or Apple login and said those login methods were paused. Later that day, @Web3Auth said it had identified a security issue in its infrastructure affecting certain embedded wallets used by Keplr. The disclosures turn a question about login availability into one about what users can still control if an account's key may also be exposed.

Web3Auth's Login Service notice dates maintenance to Nov. 6 at 18:30 UTC and attributes it to a security incident. At 13:08 UTC on Nov. 8, its status page still promised an update when service was restored. The displayed maintenance window ended Nov. 7, but that endpoint did not confirm restoration.

The Nov. 7 MetaMask Builder announcement said an initial investigation found no evidence MetaMask users were impacted.

Keplr's security documentation, updated in September 2025 and still available, says Google-login accounts receive a hexadecimal private key generated through Web3Auth instead of a recovery phrase. It says the key is encrypted and stored locally on the user's device.

The same documentation describes a View Private Key option, subject to exceptions for some legacy accounts. Keplr's mobile import guide separately documents connecting with Google and importing with a recovery phrase or private key. It also tells Google-login users to save their private key securely.

A saved key therefore matters because the documented import route uses that secret directly, separately from Google sign-in. But a menu that normally offers export does not establish that every locked-out user can reach it during this pause. The documented route is not a tested workaround for this incident.

The newer Keplr guidance makes that boundary consequential. In a later Nov. 7 update, @keplrwallet described a private-key breach affecting some social-login users and said it was assisting Web3Auth's ongoing effort to protect assets. It said accounts created with recovery phrases or hardware wallets were not affected, while Google- or Apple-login accounts might be.

For those potentially affected accounts, @keplrwallet advised against sending new funds and said remaining liquid assets should be moved to a recovery-phrase or hardware-wallet account. That guidance concerns moving assets to a different account, a separate action from loading the same key into another app.

Importing the same private key into another interface can change how its owner accesses the account. It does not change the secret authorizing it or establish that nobody else possesses it. Portability can address an access dependency without resolving a confidentiality problem.

Keplr documents independent private-key import. A different access route still uses the same secret controlling the account.

A key already usable in the existing wallet creates a different access condition from needing a fresh provider login; the documents do not test incident-time behavior.

Keplr documents private-key import separately from Google sign-in. This compares access routes; it is not an instruction to re-import an affected account.

Keplr reported Google and Apple login paused on Nov. 7, 2026. These documents do not establish independent key retrieval for someone needing a fresh sign-in.

For potentially affected accounts, Keplr's incident guidance takes precedence over routine re-import instructions.

Keplr's documentation was updated in September 2025. These paths do not verify current export availability or account safety; key exposure and asset recovery remain incident-specific.

Qualitative dependency diagrams from Keplr's documented key storage/import routes, the current Flutter SDK documentation and attributed November 7 incident statements. No loss estimates, exploit reconstruction or numerical geometry.

Keplr's documentation was updated in September 2025. These paths do not verify current export availability or account safety; key exposure and asset recovery remain incident-specific.

@Web3Auth also said it and partners had begun whitehat recovery activities targeting vulnerable Keplr wallets not yet impacted, using the original attacker's path. Separately, @keplrwallet said some reduced or empty balances could reflect mitigation efforts and that staked and locked assets made the work more complex. An unfamiliar transfer or diminished balance alone cannot establish which activity occurred.

Earlier CryptoSlate coverage of the Ledger/Cosmos access outage and Phantom's Sui cutoff showed how wallet interfaces can constrain practical access.

The useful test for a seedless wallet is whether its user has a usable, independent access path before a provider becomes unavailable. During this incident, that question sits alongside unresolved account-level impact and asset recovery. Restoring login service would answer the availability question; it would not, by itself, settle either of the others.

The Business Platinum Card® from American Express

As High As 300,000 points. Find Out Your Offer.

As High As 175,000 points. Find Out Your Offer.

Chase Ultimate Rewards is one of the most valuable and flexible rewards currencies — but how you redeem your points matters just as much as earning them.

The Chase Travel℠ portal can be an easy way to book flights, hotels, cruises and more using points or cash. It can be the best option when award space is limited or cash fares are low. However, it’s not always the best deal.

Here’s how Chase Travel works and when to use it.

Chase Travel is an online booking platform where you can search and book flights, hotels, rental cars, cruises, activities and tours using either cash, points or a combination of both.

Like an online travel agency (think Expedia), it gives you access to a wide range of travel options — but with the added benefit of earning or redeeming Chase Ultimate Rewards points.

There are three main reasons to use Chase Travel:

That said, you’ll typically get the most value from Chase points by transferring them to partners like United MileagePlus and World of Hyatt, so it’s always worth comparing options.

You don’t need a premium card to use Chase Travel — but they do unlock the highest value.

*Depending on the specific redemption, see your rewards program agreement for full details.

Because Chase lets you pool points across cards, many travelers pair a no-annual-fee card with a Sapphire card to maximize value.

Related: Better together: The ultimate guide to the best credit card combinations

Points Boost adds a layer to Chase Travel redemptions by increasing the value of points on select bookings.

Instead of relying solely on fixed redemption rates, you can sometimes get a higher value depending on the itinerary and offer available based on the card you hold.

Use TPG’s November 2026 valuation of Chase points at 2.05 cents per point as your benchmark:

This makes it essential to check each booking rather than assuming a fixed value.

Related: New favorite Chase Sapphire Reserve benefit: Stack your hotel credit with Points Boost

The Chase Travel portal works best in a few specific situations.

TPG analysis has found that flights booked through Chase's portal average 6% higher than booking directly, making it important to compare before booking.

Related: When to transfer Chase points instead of booking a hotel through Chase Travel

Booking through Chase Travel is straightforward:

You can also book travel for others, not just yourself.

One of the biggest advantages of booking flights through the portal is that you’ll typically still earn airline miles and elite status credits, since the ticket is treated like a paid fare. That makes the portal especially useful when award seats aren’t available or when fares are inexpensive.

Booking flights through the portal works similarly to Google Flights or Expedia.

You can compare airlines, review different fare types and select add-ons before heading to checkout.

As always, it’s worth comparing prices across multiple platforms, including directly with the airline.

Hotel bookings follow the same process but come with a few trade-offs.

The portal can be a great way to earn Chase points on stays at independent or boutique properties, and Points Boost can occasionally improve redemption value. However, most bookings are treated as third-party reservations, which means you typically won’t earn hotel points, elite night credits or receive status perks.

There is one notable exception: The Edit by Chase Travel, which offers benefits like room upgrades, property credits and late checkout at select luxury hotels. You'll be able to double-dip on earning Chase points and hotel loyalty points on these bookings (except Small Luxury Hotels properties, part of Hilton).

and cardholders can unlock additional value on hotel bookings, including:

And cardholders receive up to $100 in annual statement credits for hotel reservations made through Chase Travel.

Related: Book low-end or luxury hotels to get the best value from your points

Chase Travel also lets you book rental cars, cruises, tours and activities, making it possible to plan nearly an entire trip in one place.

Rental cars follow a similar booking process to flights and hotels, and Sapphire cardholders still benefit from primary rental car insurance when paying with their card — a valuable perk that can save money on coverage.

Cruises are also available to search and book through the portal, with access to thousands of itineraries across a range of cruise lines. You can typically choose to pay with cash, points or a combination of both, and some sailings may include additional perks through Chase Travel.

You can also book activities such as guided tours and experiences, which can be a convenient way to use leftover points or round out a trip.

In general, these types of bookings offer lower cents-per-point value than flights or hotels, so they’re best used for convenience rather than maximizing redemption value.

Related: Save money on your next trip: Use these tips to never pay full price for a rental car

Before booking through Chase Travel, keep a few key factors in mind.

Most importantly, always compare portal bookings with transfer partner options — particularly for premium flights and Hyatt stays.

Chase Travel is a flexible way to redeem Ultimate Rewards points, especially with Points Boost and expanded booking options across travel types. But it’s not a simple fixed-value proposition.

Used strategically, the portal can help you stretch your points and unlock trips you wouldn’t otherwise be able to book.

Gary Lineker revealed how Sheffield Wednesday icon shaped Spurs decision

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Gary Lineker has a Sheffield Wednesday legend to thank for shaping a significant part of his playing career and identity.

The now 65-year-old first came through with Leicester City in the late 1970s before playing for Everton and Barcelona.

The striker came close to breaking the record for goals scored for the England men’s national team, finding the back of the net on 48 occasions from 80 caps.

And in 1989, he returned to English football with a move to Tottenham Hotspur, and he credited a surprising figure for the decision to sign for the London outfit.

Speaking on the Match of the Day Top 10 podcast in 2021, the former BBC presenter revealed that getting the opportunity to play with Chris Waddle was a major factor in his decision to sign for Spurs.

However, he claimed that it was only after he’d agreed to the deal that his agent informed him that the former Sheffield Wednesday star had already signed for Marseille in a big-money move.

“He didn't look like a footballer, but I loved playing with him,” said Lineker, via the BBC.

Ali Al-Hamadi has reflected on his start to life at Sheffield Wednesday on social media, with his team-mate Barry Bannan reacting to the post.

“He was one of the main reasons I signed for Spurs when I left Barcelona.

“I did the deal and I was on holiday in the Caribbean and I got a phone call from my agent saying that he'd been sold to Marseille for £15 million.

“It was like someone just snatching 15 goals a season out of my pocket.

“But he is loved in Marseille, he's a proper cult hero there, he's one of their most popular players in their history.

“And he was the only person who could share a room with Gazza!”

Lineker still went through with the move to White Hart Lane, where he spent three years before departing English football again for a move to Japan with Nagoya Grampus.

During his time with Spurs, the forward scored 67 times from 105 appearances, winning the FA Cup in the process.

Lineker cited Waddle as a major inspiration for his decision to sign for Spurs, but perhaps it would’ve gone differently had he known about his move to Marseille.

The 65-year-old revealed in 2015 that he also had the option to join Manchester United in 1989, who were then managed by Alex Ferguson.

The Scot wanted to partner Lineker with Mark Hughes, with the club targeting a first league title since 1967.

Of course, the Red Devils went on to become a juggernaut in the 1990s, and the former England star must wonder what could’ve been had it not been for Waddle.

It was only after his time at Marseille that Waddle cemented himself as an icon at Sheffield Wednesday.

After three years in France, the playmaker made the switch to Hillsborough ahead of the first ever Premier League season in 1992.

How good was Chris Waddle for Sheffield Wednesday? Comment your opinion below

The now 65-year-old spent four seasons with the Owls, where he made 109 league appearances, contributing 10 goals and 22 assists, with the club finishing seventh, seventh, 13th and 15th in the table.

Even by the standards of someone in the latter stages of his career, Waddle’s performances for the Yorkshire outfit remained quite impressive, and he had a strong final year at Hillsborough, scoring two and assisting six in the Premier League at the age of 34.

While Lineker was unable to enjoy a spell at Spurs with Waddle as his teammate, the pair did, of course, feature together at international level.

In particular, the 1990 World Cup in Italy was a highlight of their time together, with the former Sheffield Wednesday player enjoying a strong tournament that ultimately ended up in disappointment in the penalty shootout in the semi-finals.

That tournament especially highlighted to Spurs fans what could’ve been with Lineker and Waddle working together, as it was England’s best ever showing in the competition on foreign soil until this year’s edition in North America.

Why Kasper Hogh would not have taken Celtic penalty as Mika Baur injury addressed

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Martin O’Neill admitted he would not have let Kasper Hogh take a second half penalty that Benjamin Nygren ended up failing to score in any case as Celtic squeezed past Kilmarnock at Parkhead.

The striker’s goal drought was extended to 16 games and he’s still to open his account at home for his new club. Defender Liam Scales was the match winner after converting a cross from Camilo Dusan in the 25th minute.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

Hogh had earlier endured the heartbreak of seeing a goal chalked off for a foul committed earlier in the move by Mika Baur and he snatched at a chance shortly afterwards when one-on-one with Kilmarnock ‘keeper Nico Mantl.

Hogh worked hard and was named man-of-the-match but was replaced after 70 minutes. Celtic were awarded a penalty shortly afterwards and many Celtic fans lamented Hogh not still being on the pitch to take it. However, O’Neill confirmed this wouldn’t have happened even if he had been available.

Nygren duly saw Mantl make an excellent save from his effort from 12 yards to keep Kilmarnock in the game. The visitors came close to equalising right at the death when Ieuan Owen failed to connect with a cross right in front of goal.

“We all thought Kasper had got off the mark,” said O’Neill, with reference to the goal that wasn’t. “We all thought that for about 35 seconds. I think myself that maybe he would have scored the next one had the first one counted but he worked exceptionally hard for us. I was pleased the team dug it out.

“We got the penalty but had he still been on the pitch I would not have asked him to take it. He might have missed. As it turned out, we did miss.

“In front of goal, it is proving a difficult one for him,” O'Neill added. “But he has worked exceptionally hard in the game, as he did on Thursday night (v Besiktas). I think things will come, honestly. He thought he had broken the spell but his hold up play was much, much better.”

Hogh received a warm ovation when he was replaced after 70 minutes by James Forrest. “I think that’s encouraging,” said O’Neill. “If you have the crowd behind you, if you’re working hard, they will forgive some other things. Now I am not saying that will last forever….Of course strikers live and die by their goals. But he feels we all believe in him in the dressing room, that’s encouraging for him.”

O’Neill stressed he had not seen a replay of the incident involving Baur that meant Hogh’s goal after 13 minutes was ruled off after a VAR check. Referee John Beaton was called to the monitor and he very abruptly signalled he was disallowing the strike.

“I have genuinely not seen it back because the little screen we have on the touchline is too small for me,” he said. “I can only go with what the lads were saying, they thought it was touch and go.”

O’Neill also revealed that Baur, who limped off near the end, had suffered ankle damage and has been booked in for a scan. The midfielder is due to join up with the Germany Under-21s in the international break.

“He’s in the treatment room,” he said. “His ankle is really sore and we don’t know the damage. We’ll wait until tomorrow to get a scan. Gosh, I’d hope he won’t be out terribly long but I don't know at the moment. He’s been great for us considering he’s not the tallest. He’d be about ten stone maybe not even that but his work rate is incredible. His overall reading of the game is really good and he’s not frightened.”

Bigger than Godzilla!

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Fortescue’s plans for the Pilbara include wind turbine blades that are bigger than Godzilla.

While Fortescue’s boss hobnobs with King Charles III and chats about sustainability, under the Commonwealth Nature Initiative, progress continues towards real zero in the remote Pilbara of Western Australia. See the video of Andrew and Charles here.

As part of Dr Andrew Forrest’s vision of real zero by 2030, Fortescue is installing wind turbines with a blade length of 89 metres. Bigger than Godzilla! Have we got your attention now? The blades will be deployed at the Nullagine Wind Farm in the Pilbara region of Western Australia. They will arrive by ship at the Port Headland project site. Fortescue has done major upgrades to allow the blades easy access and to minimise impact on local traffic. The community will also receive regular updates throughout the delivery program to keep residents informed.

For global readers, I would like to point out that Fortescue’s mining operations and land tenements in the Pilbara region of Western Australia cover more than 87,000 square kilometres (34,000 square miles). This is roughly the size of Austria (83,879 km²), Ireland (84,421 km²), or Serbia (88,361 km²), and more than twice the size of countries like the Netherlands (41,850 km²) or Switzerland (41,285 km²). And for USA readers, Fortescue’s land holdings alone are larger than South Carolina, West Virginia, or Maryland, and roughly the same size as the entire state of Maine or Indiana. But only one eighth the size of Texas.

You will have read about Twiggy Forrest’s achievements and vision of real zero mining operations here and here. Fortescue is taking steps to educate the public on decarbonisation. You need to take the people with you, especially the shareholders. Here is a great video produced by Fortescue to show how to decode decarbonisation. The Godzilla-sized wind turbines are part of an ecosystem. They will provide the power to recharge the electric dump trucks. But just how do you recharge a 240 ton dump truck in a timely manner? Using 6MW robotic fast charging, of course, thus eliminating 26% of Fortescue’s emissions — and saving a dump truck of money in the process.

CleanTechnica reported on the acquisition of Williams Racing 5 years ago, in order for Fortescue to access Williams’ fast-charging technology. It has taken some time, but it looks like everything is coming together. I still have the picture in my head, inspired by this acquisition, of huge dump trucks doing F1 races in the red dust of the Australian Outback. Beyond the fantasies of Mad Max, even. While we are indulging in mental gymnastics, the Fortescue video tells us that 6MW of charging is like boiling 1,875 electric kettles all at once. That’s a lot of tea or coffee! A less than 30-minute charge will give 4 hours of operation for the truck. The charging equipment has to be resilient, as the Pilbara is hot and dusty, with temperatures in summer over 40 degrees and down to below freezing on winter nights.

Episode 2 answers the question “Can a green grid power a mining operation 24/7?” It’s a big ask. Fortescue is the fourth largest iron ore producer in the world, exporting 200 million tons a year. The advantages: Fortescue achieves cost savings, does good for the climate, and keeps control of its energy. In the current global situation, I would add energy security.

The Pilbara has 300 days a year of irradiation and Fortescue plans to harvest that with a planned 1.5 gigawatts of solar farms. Eventually, this will be backed up with 900 MW wind and balanced by 5 GWh of battery storage — enough electricity to power 800,000 homes on solar alone. This is all connected by a high-voltage green grid consisting of 600 km of transmission lines. “A city scale power system in the middle of nowhere.” The green operation does not draw from the public grid; not the distant future, but right now.

Fortescue is not just content to reduce emissions for its mining operations; it is also working on green steel — a product for the future. Fortescue reports that it has produced its first hot metal via an electric smelting process in a pilot project at Christmas Creek. Initially using a blended feedstock, as the commissioning process continues, it is expected to be able to handle Pilbara iron ore. Larger scale production is the aim. At the moment, the focus is on testing, refining, and optimising the process.

Fortescue Metals Chief Executive Officer Dino Otranto said: “This is a significant milestone for our Green Metal Project and another step towards producing commercial-scale green metal in Australia.

“For decades Australia has exported iron ore to the world. The next opportunity is to create more value from that ore by producing green metal here at home.

“The real opportunity goes beyond green metal. It’s about building a new industrial economy around Australia’s renewable energy advantage.

“Nobody has solved green metal production using Pilbara ore at commercial scale yet. That’s the challenge we’re taking on.”

I’m looking forward to more good news of progress towards real zero from Fortescue Metals. Have CleanTechnica readers noticed any other mining companies heading down the same path? If so, make a comment below. Even in the “hard to decarbonise” mining sector, electricity sourced from renewables and stored in batteries is the way to go for cost saving, climate protection, and keeping control of energy inputs. In Australia’s Pilbara, the future is bright and electric.

David Waterworth is a retired teacher who divides his time between looking after his grandchildren and trying to make sure they have a planet to live on. He is long on Tesla [NASDAQ:TSLA].

'Provoked' - Brentford's Keith Andrews makes Chema Andres claim after Igor Thiago red card at Brighton

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Brentford boss Keith Andrews questioned the use of VAR in the Premier League after Igor Thiago’s first-half dismissal proved costly in a 2-0 defeat at high-flying Brighton.

Bees striker Thiago was initially shown a yellow card by referee Michael Oliver after reacting to a shirt pull and subsequent push from Albion midfielder Chema Andres, who was also booked.

Did you know with an ad-lite subscription to SussexWorld, you get 70% fewer ads while viewing the news that matters to you.

But the punishment was upgraded to red on review as match official Oliver deemed the Brazil international used “force” to push his opponent in the face with a raised arm.

Brentford’s only other league defeat this term came after Yehor Yarmoliuk was shown a first-half red card in last month’s 2-1 home loss to Liverpool.

Andrews accepted his players must take responsibility for their conduct but questioned the circumstances surrounding both incidents.

“We’ve lost two games in the league this season and both have been when we’ve gone down to 10 men, so you need to analyse yourself first clearly,” he said.

“On both scenarios, we can do better. On both scenarios, the opposition player has been the one to provoke the situation and antagonise our player and react towards our player in an aggressive way.

“Players’ reactions now mean everything. I’m going to reiterate it: we should do better. But the game is in a state now of a place I don’t particularly like. We’ll have to have a look at that because it’s costing us.”

Brentford were already trailing at the time of Thiago’s sending-off following a stunning long-range opener from Brighton midfielder Diego Gomez.

Disgruntled away fans chanted “2-0 to the referee” and “Michael Oliver, it’s all about you” after Pascal Gross doubled Albion’s lead with a 42nd-minute penalty awarded for Keane Lewis-Potter’s shirt pull on Luka Vuskovic.

Andrews, who felt his side should have been “cuter” in the penalty incident, was frustrated Oliver was sent to the pitch-side monitor before changing his initial decision on Thiago.

“I’m struggling to believe in this day and age where we see the referee’s call on pitch and the bar of where they’re given the licence to referee in-game, which is what I should think it should be, but he’s been sent to the screen,” he said.

“We play on the edge, we’re a team that likes to play on the edge, I want us to be on the edge. But it clearly cost us.”

Brighton climbed to second after marking their return to the Amex Stadium following five successive away matches with a comfortable victory.

Gomez lashed into the top-left corner with his left foot to follow up his goal from the halfway line in last month’s 5-0 thrashing of rivals Crystal Palace.

Seagulls head coach Fabian Hurzeler said: “It’s a funny story because yesterday he trained shooting with the left foot and I couldn’t see him scoring today with this left foot – the shots weren’t great.

“I asked him after the game where he got this quality from overnight. It’s a huge improvement in a very short period.”

Albion’s win was a seventh from 10 top-flight games and lifted them above Manchester City and back to within five points of leaders Arsenal.

Asked when Seagulls supporters can start getting really excited, Hurzeler said: “They should be always excited if they watch our game, and that’s our job to make them excited.

“I always say they can dream, they can have high expectations on us, but these are things we can’t control.”

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Bloomberg’s Mark Gurman says CEO John Ternus is pressuring AI czar Craig Federighi to accelerate the Siri roadmap and deliver a powerful AI agent that can compete with OpenAI’s Dots or Meta’s Muse. While Federighi has delivered a functional version of Siri AI on an incredibly short timeline, the company has no illusions that it’s keeping pace with its rivals.

Two significant improvements are expected to be made to Siri in the coming months. One will give the assistant fine-grained control over Apple’s first-party apps, allowing it to do things like rotate and crop images in Photos and organize Safari tabs. Those capabilities are expected to land with iOS 27.3. The bigger change, integration with third-party apps, is likely to land later, in the Spring, according to Gurman. That would let Siri place an order through Grubhub or book a dinner reservation through OpenTable, for example.

But that’s just the start. Apple is expected to push Siri to become more agentic and enable features that will allow it to perform ongoing tasks, not just one-offs. Gurman says:

Dots, Muse and Grok can keep track of goals, determine what needs to happen next and continue pursuing objectives without the user initiating every individual step. Apple ultimately wants to go in this direction, I’m told, and I believe the company could make Siri more like an agent as early as next year — not that it has much of a choice, given current consumer and market demands.

Grab This Packet Of Seasoning To Transform Store-Bought Snacks Into A Delicious Masterpiece

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Many kitchens contain a well-stocked, if not cluttered, spice cabinet, making it easy to grab what you need when a recipe calls for spices. However, don't rule out using those jarred seasonings with store-bought snacks, too — taco seasoning is one such ideal candidate.

This savory spice blend is one of the kitchen's most versatile, useful in everything from elevating your grilled burgers to giving chicken tenders a delicious twist. Bringing together flavors like earthy ground cumin, sweet paprika, onion and chili powders, oregano, salt, and more, it's a testament to the idea that a good spice blend is greater than the sum of its parts. All the more reason to throw it on your favorite snack. Think the crunchy classics: Chex Mix, fresh or bagged popcorn, pretzels, a nut mix, or good ol' chips.

To make sure the seasoning sticks, you'll want to apply some heat and fat, not just sprinkle on the spices. Fold a packet into some tasty melted butter or coconut oil, then mix it into your snack of choice. Bake at around 350 degrees Fahrenheit, stirring occasionally, until you have a delectably browned and seasoned snack mix.

Seasonings are made for modifications. So if a simple dusting of taco seasoning isn't what you're craving, it's easy to fine-tune the flavors to your liking. An easy starting point is a sweet element — honey, agave syrup, or even maple syrup all pair fabulously with taco seasoning. Simply mix the sugary ingredient into the fat to lend the spiced mix an added dimension.

Alternatively, customize the flavors by mixing up the spice blend yourself. Taco seasoning can contain a variety of ingredients in numerous styles, so brighten up your snack seasoning with grated lime zest, or turn up the heat with cayenne or ground árbol chilies. Consider even amplifying the warmth with cinnamon or allspice — ingredients that'll pair terrifically with sweet flavors.

Alongside such reworked blends, keep expanding your store-bought snack repertoire. Throw in a bag of dry-roasted edamame beans, some Fritos, or plantain chips, or buy some chicharrones (fried pork rinds). Mix and match nibbles to your liking, dust with taco seasoning, then enjoy a new world of snackable possibilities.

Aston Villa twice led by two goals but Manchester United produced two spirited fightbacks to avoid defeat and potentially prolong Carrick’s reign as manager.

Goals from Brazilian winger Alysson and Senegal striker Nicolas Jackson put Villa 2-0 up inside five minutes but the hosts were level by half-time thanks to Bruno Fernandes’ penalty and Youri Tielemans equaliser against his former club.

‘What are we expecting to change over the next few weeks and months? What are they working on? They need to do a lot better than that.’

Del Casher, Guitarist Who Invented the Wah-Wah Pedal, Dead at 88

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Del Casher, the guitarist and inventor whose innovations included the wah-wah pedal, has died at the age of 88.

Casher died Oct. 1 following a battle with cancer, according to a GoFundMe to raise money for his widow, Eiko. TMZ was first to report on Casher’s death.

Born Delton Kacher in Hammond, Indiana, Casher first performed with artists like Gene Autry, Lawrence Welk, and the Three Suns, while also experimenting with new techniques and technologies in an effort to make his own electric-guitar playing stand out.

In the early Sixties, while playing with the Thomas Organ Company’s Vox Ampliphonic Orchestra, Casher asked about making his guitar sound like the trumpet, complete with the ability to mildly suppress the instrument mid-playing. While Casher often credited Thomas Organ’s Brad Plunkett as the engineer of the device, the wah-wah pedal was considered Casher’s brainchild.

First dubbed the “Cry Baby” pedal, Casher also recorded a demo for instrument stores showcasing how to use the pedal, as well as an accompanying single. Both the pedal and the single failed to catch on with the music-playing public upon release in 1967.

However, Frank Zappa, who used the wah-wah pedal in 1966 when Casher sat in with the Mothers of Invention, gifted a pedal to Jimi Hendrix, solidifying the wah-wah’s place in music history soon after.

Hendrix would employ the pedal throughout his classic Electric Ladyland — including on “Voodoo Child,” “Burning of the Midnight Lamp,” and “All Along the Watchtower” — while fellow guitarist Eric Clapton wah-wah’d on Cream tracks like “Tales of Brave Ulysses.”

Soon after, many of the great rock guitarists of the era, like Jimmy Page, Tony Iommi, David Gilmour, and more, were experimenting with the wah-wah pedal. By 1971, the wah-wah pedal had featured on a Number One single and Oscar-winning song thanks to its use on Isaac Hayes’ “Theme From Shaft.” Generations of guitar players have used the pedal since. Trending Stories U2 Sound Revitalized on 'Carnaval de Luz' Del Casher, Guitarist Who Invented the Wah-Wah Pedal, Dead at 88 The Teenagers Taking a Cancer Drug to Get Taller Good Riddance to the Cybertruck, the Chariot of the Dumbest Gods

Outside of inventing — Casher is also credited with contributing to the creation of the Ecco-Fonic and the Fender Electronic Echo Chamber — the guitarist also found work with artists like Elvis Presley, appearing in his 1964 film Roustabout. Casher also contributed film scores later in his career.

“I feel gratified that I was there at the beginning,” Casher told The New York Times. “I would just like guitar players to think of me once in a while. The only thing I ask is: remember me.”
